# Daily Summary: 2025-03-21

### SemEval-2025 Task 1: AdMIRe -- Advancing Multimodal Idiomaticity Representation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15358v1)
- **Authors**: Thomas Pickard, Aline Villavicencio, Maggie Mi, Wei He, Dylan Phelps, Carolina Scarton, Marco Idiart
- **Abstract**: Idiomatic expressions present a unique challenge in NLP, as their meanings are often not directly inferable from their constituent words. Despite recent advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs), idiomaticity remains a significant obstacle to robust semantic representation. We present datasets and tasks for SemEval-2025 Task 1: AdMiRe (Advancing Multimodal Idiomaticity Representation), which challenges the community to assess and improve models' ability to interpret idiomatic expressions in multimodal contexts and in multiple languages. Participants competed in two subtasks: ranking images based on their alignment with idiomatic or literal meanings, and predicting the next image in a sequence. The most effective methods achieved human-level performance by leveraging pretrained LLMs and vision-language models in mixture-of-experts settings, with multiple queries used to smooth over the weaknesses in these models' representations of idiomaticity.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the SemEval-2025 Task 1 paper:

**Summary:**

The paper describes SemEval-2025 Task 1, "AdMIRe - Advancing Multimodal Idiomaticity Representation." This task focuses on evaluating how well NLP models, particularly large language models (LLMs) and vision-language models (VLMs), can understand and represent idiomatic expressions in multimodal contexts (images and image sequences) and across multiple languages (English and Brazilian Portuguese).  The task consists of two subtasks: (A) ranking images based on their alignment with either the literal or idiomatic meaning of a noun compound given a context sentence, and (B) predicting the next image in a sequence representing either the literal or idiomatic meaning. The dataset includes static images with generated captions, and image sequences (three-panel comic-strip like data). Evaluation metrics include top-image accuracy, discounted cumulative gain (DCG) for ranking, and completion and sentence-type accuracy for image sequences. The paper also outlines the approaches used by participating teams, highlighting the use of LLMs, VLMs, and various prompting and fine-tuning techniques. The paper further includes the results from system participation as well as human annotations for an understanding of the task by human annotators.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The task design itself is moderately novel.  While existing work has explored idiomaticity detection and multimodal representation, the combination of: 1) ranking images based on idiomatic/literal alignment, 2) predicting the next image in a *sequence*, and 3) evaluating across both English and Brazilian Portuguese represents a unique challenge. The emphasis on image sequences as capturing temporal aspects of idioms is a valuable addition.  However, the *concepts* are largely built on existing work.

* **Significance:** The task addresses a critical limitation of current LLMs: their struggle with figurative language. By explicitly focusing on idiomatic expressions in multimodal settings, the task encourages the development of more robust and nuanced semantic representations.  The results reveal that even with considerable effort, LLMs and VLMs often require significant prompting and fine-tuning to achieve human-level performance, underscoring the ongoing challenge. The creation of the AdMIRe dataset, even with its limited size, is a valuable resource for future research. Furthermore the exploration of system types used by teams from solely text, to image/text can provide insights into which modalities are more helpful.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Well-defined Task:** The task and subtasks are clearly articulated, with appropriate evaluation metrics.
    * **Multimodal Focus:** The integration of visual information into the idiomaticity task is a significant strength, reflecting the importance of context in understanding figurative language.
    * **Multilingual Aspect:** Including Brazilian Portuguese expands the scope beyond primarily English-centric NLP.
    * **Analysis of System Approaches:** The paper provides a good overview of the methods employed by participating teams, highlighting common strategies and challenges. This section is useful for understanding current trends in the field.
    * **Baseline comparison with Human annotators:** The analysis compared systems to human annotators to get a better understanding of the ability of such systems.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Dataset Size:** The relatively small size of the AdMIRe dataset (especially the subtask B data) is a limitation. While the authors explain their reasoning for manual curation, a larger dataset would improve the generalizability of the results.
    * **Reliance on Generated Images:** The use of AI-generated images, while practical, introduces potential biases. The generated data is inherently tied to the capabilities and limitations of the specific models used.
    * **Potentially Inconsistent Difficulty of Literal/Figurative Images:** The task assumes an ordering of images representing different degrees of idiomaticity/literalness. While efforts were made to ensure this, the subjective nature of image ranking could introduce noise into the evaluation.
    * **Limited diversity of Cultural background**: Due to the dataset being created by middle-class developers, the dataset does not account for a wide-range of cultural understanding and diversity.
    * **Over-reliance on LLMs:** While LLMs are state-of-the-art, the task submissions indicate an over-reliance on the model architectures potentially limiting the exploration of other methods for this task.

* **Impact:** The SemEval task is likely to have a modest impact on the field by:
    * Providing a benchmark and a dataset for evaluating idiomaticity representation in multimodal settings.
    * Encouraging further research on how to improve LLMs' ability to handle figurative language.
    * Highlighting the importance of multimodal and multilingual approaches to NLP.

**Overall:**

The paper presents a worthwhile contribution to the field of computational linguistics by introducing a new SemEval task focused on a challenging and important problem: idiomaticity representation in multimodal and multilingual contexts. While the dataset size and reliance on generated images are limitations, the task design, analysis of system approaches, and insights gained about LLMs' challenges with figurative language make this a valuable contribution.

**Score: 6.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### EfficientLLaVA:Generalizable Auto-Pruning for Large Vision-language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15369v1)
- **Authors**: Yinan Liang, Ziwei Wang, Xiuwei Xu, Jie Zhou, Jiwen Lu
- **Abstract**: While multimodal large language models demonstrate strong performance in complex reasoning tasks, they pose significant challenges related to model complexity during deployment, especially for resource-limited devices. In this paper, we propose an automatic pruning method for large vision-language models to enhance the efficiency of multimodal reasoning. Conventional methods rely on the training data of the original model to select the proper pruning ratio for different network components. However, these methods are impractical for large vision-language models due to the unaffordable search costs caused by web-scale training corpus. In contrast, our approach only leverages a small number of samples to search for the desired pruning policy by maximizing its generalization ability on unknown training data while maintaining the model accuracy, which enables the achievement of an optimal trade-off between accuracy and efficiency for large visual language models. Specifically, we formulate the generalization gap of the pruning strategy using the structural risk minimization principle. Based on both task performance and generalization capability, we iteratively search for the optimal pruning policy within a given search space and optimize the vision projector to evolve the search space with higher upper bound of performance. We conduct extensive experiments on the ScienceQA, Vizwiz, MM-vet, and LLaVA-Bench datasets for the task of visual question answering. Using only 64 samples for pruning policy search, EfficientLLaVA achieves an accuracy of 83.05% on ScienceQA, along with a $\times$ 1.8 speedup compared to the dense LLaVA-v1.5-7B model.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EfficientLLaVA, an automatic pruning method tailored for large vision-language models (LVLMs). It aims to reduce model complexity and improve inference speed on resource-constrained devices while preserving performance. Unlike traditional pruning methods that require large-scale training data for policy search, EfficientLLaVA uses only a small number of samples. The method formulates the generalization gap of the pruning policy using structural risk minimization (SRM) and iteratively searches for the optimal pruning policy by optimizing the vision projector and pruning the weights based on task performance and generalization ability. Experiments on ScienceQA, VizWiz, MM-vet, and LLaVA-Bench demonstrate that EfficientLLaVA achieves a significant speedup with minimal accuracy loss compared to baseline models like LLaVA.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its efficient approach to pruning LVLMs by leveraging structural risk minimization to maximize the generalization ability of the pruning policy using only a small number of samples. This is particularly significant as it addresses the impracticality of using web-scale training data for pruning policy search in large multimodal models. The iterative search space evolution by optimizing the vision projector further enhances its contribution. While post-training quantization and pruning are established techniques, the specific application of SRM and search space evolution within the context of LVLM pruning represents a valuable contribution.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work stems from its potential to enable the deployment of complex LVLMs on resource-constrained devices. EfficientLLaVA reduces model complexity and inference latency, making these models more accessible for real-world applications, especially in scenarios where computational resources are limited. Demonstrating a tangible speedup without significant accuracy loss on standard benchmarks highlights the practical relevance of this work. The ability to adapt to unseen datasets is also an important outcome. The study shows how to create a reasonable efficiency/performance tradeoff for large vision language models.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Efficient Pruning:** The method effectively reduces the search cost for the optimal pruning policy by using small proxy samples, addressing the computational limitations of large models.
    *   **Generalization Ability:** The use of structural risk minimization is well-reasoned and helps to improve the generalization of the pruning policy to unseen data.
    *   **Search Space Evolution:** Iteratively improving the pruning space by optimizing projector weights is a novel and effective approach.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental results demonstrate significant speedups with minimal accuracy loss on multiple datasets, supporting the effectiveness of the proposed method. The ablation studies analyze different components of the proposed framework.
    *   **Clarity:** The paper is generally well-written and clearly explains the proposed method and experimental setup.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Comparisons:** While the comparisons to LLM-Pruner and SparseGPT are valuable, the paper could benefit from comparisons to a wider range of existing pruning methods, particularly those specifically designed for transformers.
    *   **Evaluation Datasets:** While the paper evaluates EfficientLLaVA on ScienceQA, VizWiz, MM-vet, and LLaVA-Bench, a broader evaluation on diverse VQA datasets could further solidify its effectiveness and generalizability.
    *   **Hardware Details:** The paper lacks some details about the precise hardware and experimental setup used for the CPU inference speed tests. The exact CPU model and memory configuration used will provide more meaningful analysis of inference results.
    *   **Scaling:** While the approach shows promise in a 7B model, the impact on larger models (e.g., models with > 10B parameters) is unknown. Experiments on larger models would further demonstrate the scalability and effectiveness of EfficientLLaVA.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work has the potential to influence the field by providing a more efficient and practical approach to pruning large vision-language models. It could inspire further research on applying structural risk minimization and other optimization techniques to improve the efficiency and generalizability of pruning methods for large AI models.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and practically significant approach to pruning LVLMs. The method's efficiency, generalization ability, and iterative search space evolution contribute to its overall effectiveness. The experimental results are promising and demonstrate the potential for deployment on resource-constrained devices. However, the paper's value would be improved by broader dataset comparisons, additional details about the experimental setup, and experiments with larger models. Given the impact of large model pruning, the practicality for downstream tasks with limited computing resources, this work presents a worthwhile contribution to the field.

### CCDP: Composition of Conditional Diffusion Policies with Guided Sampling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15386v1)
- **Authors**: Amirreza Razmjoo, Sylvain Calinon, Michael Gienger, Fan Zhang
- **Abstract**: Imitation Learning offers a promising approach to learn directly from data without requiring explicit models, simulations, or detailed task definitions. During inference, actions are sampled from the learned distribution and executed on the robot. However, sampled actions may fail for various reasons, and simply repeating the sampling step until a successful action is obtained can be inefficient. In this work, we propose an enhanced sampling strategy that refines the sampling distribution to avoid previously unsuccessful actions. We demonstrate that by solely utilizing data from successful demonstrations, our method can infer recovery actions without the need for additional exploratory behavior or a high-level controller. Furthermore, we leverage the concept of diffusion model decomposition to break down the primary problem (which may require long-horizon history to manage failures) into multiple smaller, more manageable sub-problems in learning, data collection, and inference, thereby enabling the system to adapt to variable failure counts. Our approach yields a low-level controller that dynamically adjusts its sampling space to improve efficiency when prior samples fall short. We validate our method across several tasks, including door opening with unknown directions, object manipulation, and button-searching scenarios, demonstrating that our approach outperforms traditional baselines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "CCDP: Composition of Conditional Diffusion Policies with Guided Sampling":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CCDP, a novel method to enhance diffusion policies for robot imitation learning by incorporating a failure recovery mechanism directly within the low-level controller. CCDP addresses the challenge of robots failing to execute sampled actions by refining the sampling distribution to avoid previously unsuccessful actions. It leverages successful demonstration data to infer recovery actions without explicit exploration or high-level control.  CCDP uses diffusion model decomposition to manage long-horizon histories required for failure recovery, breaking down the problem into smaller, manageable sub-problems. This enables dynamic adjustment of the sampling space to improve efficiency when prior samples fall short. The method is validated across various tasks (door opening, object manipulation, button searching) and demonstrates superior performance compared to standard diffusion policies and a baseline involving rejection sampling.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper exhibits moderate novelty. While diffusion policies for imitation learning are established, the contribution lies in the *specific way* CCDP handles failure recovery.  The key novelty points are:

*   **Failure-Aware Sampling:** Moving away from blindly resampling from the original distribution to actively steering away from regions known to lead to failure based on observed history is a valuable improvement.
*   **Diffusion Model Decomposition for Failure Recovery:** The decomposition of the diffusion model into state, history, and failure components to manage complexity and improve tractability is a significant design choice. By treating failures as negative guidance rather than trying to model all possibilities, the approach provides a more efficient and adaptable solution.
*   **Data Synthesis for Recovery Dataset:** Synthesizing a recovery dataset when the demonstrations don't include explicit failure cases is a practical and important aspect.
*   **Composition of experts that doesn't require data classification:** Reducing the human burden in complex problems.

However, the novelty is somewhat limited by the reliance on pre-defined failure features and the manual selection of weights for combining the different diffusion models. The concept of composing experts for robotics is not entirely new, but the specific application and integration with diffusion policies is a worthwhile contribution.

**Significance:** The paper's significance is also moderate.

*   **Practical Applicability:** The method focuses on improving the robustness of robot policies in real-world scenarios where failures are common. This is a relevant problem.
*   **Data Efficiency:** The approach aims to learn recovery strategies from limited data (successful demonstrations only), which is a valuable asset.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The experiments across several tasks provide evidence that the method works in practice and outperforms baselines.

However, the study has limitations:

*   **Reliance on Simulation:** Demonstrations collected in simulation may not fully capture the complexities of real-world failures. The "sim2real" gap can significantly impact the effectiveness of recovery strategies.
*   **Manually Defined Features:** The need for manually defined failure features limits the autonomy and generalizability of the method. Autonomous feature extraction would be a substantial improvement.
*   **Static Failure Causes:** The assumption of failures remaining static over time is very limiting. If the environment changes or the *cause* of the failure is not static, then CCDP would be less effective.

**Strengths:**

*   Addresses a practical and important problem in robot imitation learning.
*   Presents a novel approach to failure recovery based on diffusion policies.
*   Empirically validated with promising results.
*   Focuses on data efficiency.

**Weaknesses:**

*   Relies on simulation-based demonstrations.
*   Requires manually defined failure features.
*   The assumption that the underlying cause of the failure remains static over time.
*   Manual weight tuning for diffusion model components.
*   No analysis of computational costs or scalability.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence research in robot imitation learning, particularly in areas focusing on robustness and failure recovery.  The idea of failure-aware sampling and diffusion model decomposition could inspire other researchers to explore similar approaches. However, to make a significant impact, the method needs to be extended to handle more complex, real-world scenarios with autonomous feature extraction and more robust ways to tune hyperparameters.

**Score:** 6

**Rationale:**  While the paper presents a novel and potentially useful method for failure recovery in robot imitation learning, its limitations in terms of real-world applicability, reliance on manual feature engineering, and static cause of failure prevents it from achieving a higher score. The integration of failure-aware sampling and diffusion model decomposition is a valuable contribution, but the paper's impact is constrained by these limitations. The significance is moderate due to the potential for real-world application, data efficiency, and experimental validation, but also limited because the assumptions of the environment might not hold in more complex scenarios.

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 6/10

### Improving Adversarial Transferability on Vision Transformers via Forward Propagation Refinement
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15404v1)
- **Authors**: Yuchen Ren, Zhengyu Zhao, Chenhao Lin, Bo Yang, Lu Zhou, Zhe Liu, Chao Shen
- **Abstract**: Vision Transformers (ViTs) have been widely applied in various computer vision and vision-language tasks. To gain insights into their robustness in practical scenarios, transferable adversarial examples on ViTs have been extensively studied. A typical approach to improving adversarial transferability is by refining the surrogate model. However, existing work on ViTs has restricted their surrogate refinement to backward propagation. In this work, we instead focus on Forward Propagation Refinement (FPR) and specifically refine two key modules of ViTs: attention maps and token embeddings. For attention maps, we propose Attention Map Diversification (AMD), which diversifies certain attention maps and also implicitly imposes beneficial gradient vanishing during backward propagation. For token embeddings, we propose Momentum Token Embedding (MTE), which accumulates historical token embeddings to stabilize the forward updates in both the Attention and MLP blocks. We conduct extensive experiments with adversarial examples transferred from ViTs to various CNNs and ViTs, demonstrating that our FPR outperforms the current best (backward) surrogate refinement by up to 7.0\% on average. We also validate its superiority against popular defenses and its compatibility with other transfer methods. Codes and appendix are available at https://github.com/RYC-98/FPR.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of improving the transferability of adversarial examples generated for Vision Transformers (ViTs).  It proposes a novel approach called Forward Propagation Refinement (FPR), which refines the surrogate model during the forward propagation phase, unlike existing methods that focus solely on backward propagation. FPR comprises two key components: Attention Map Diversification (AMD) to diversify attention maps and induce beneficial gradient vanishing, and Momentum Token Embedding (MTE) to stabilize token embeddings by accumulating historical information. Extensive experiments demonstrate that FPR outperforms existing backward-based surrogate refinement techniques and shows good compatibility with other transfer methods and robustness against various defenses.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the shift from backward propagation-focused refinement to forward propagation refinement for improving adversarial transferability in ViTs.  While the individual techniques (AMD and MTE) might have inspirations from other areas (e.g., momentum distillation), their application in the context of forward propagation refinement within ViTs for adversarial transferability is novel. AMD's implicit gradient vanishing effect, despite operating in the forward pass, is also a non-trivial and valuable observation.  The paper distinguishes itself clearly from previous work like PNAPO, TGR, and GNS.

**Significance:** The significance stems from addressing a crucial problem in adversarial machine learning: the limited transferability of adversarial examples. By improving transferability, the paper brings us closer to understanding and mitigating the vulnerabilities of ViTs in real-world scenarios. The improved attack success rates against defended models are particularly relevant. Moreover, the forward propagation perspective opens up new avenues for research in surrogate model refinement. The demonstration of compatibility with other transfer-based attacks makes the method a practical and versatile tool.

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Approach:**  The shift to forward propagation refinement is a conceptually valuable contribution.
*   **Clear Methodology:**  AMD and MTE are well-defined and explained. The analysis of AMD's impact on backward propagation is insightful.
*   **Extensive Experiments:**  The experiments cover a wide range of ViTs, CNNs, defenses, and transfer methods. The ablation studies provide good insight into the contribution of each component.
*   **Strong Results:**  The empirical results consistently demonstrate the superiority of FPR over existing methods.
*   **Well-Written:** The paper is generally well-written and easy to follow.
*   **Code Availability**: The availability of code enhances the reproducibility and usability of the research.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Theoretical Justification:** While AMD's implicit gradient vanishing is explained, a more rigorous theoretical analysis of *why* FPR works so effectively might be beneficial. What are the properties of forward propagation that make it a good space to perform such refinement? This would elevate the paper from an engineering success to a scientific breakthrough.
*   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** While the authors analyze hyperparameter sensitivity, the process of selecting the best hyperparameters could be better described. Is there a heuristic or a general strategy that can be applied across different models? More intuitive explanation on why each optimal value works best would be welcomed.
*   **Limited Diversity of Datasets:** The experiments were conducted with the ILSVRC2012 validation set. Additional experimentation with different dataset like CIFAR-100 or other domain would add more robust insight of the proposed algorithm.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence future research in adversarial machine learning, particularly in the area of surrogate model refinement for ViTs. The forward propagation perspective may inspire new techniques and a deeper understanding of model vulnerabilities.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to adversarial transferability for ViTs. The forward propagation perspective, the clear methodology, and the strong empirical results are definite strengths. However, the lack of deeper theoretical justification and some limitations in experimental setup prevent it from achieving a higher score. The impact of this work will depend on future research that builds upon the proposed framework and explores the full potential of forward propagation refinement.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Visual Persona: Foundation Model for Full-Body Human Customization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15406v1)
- **Authors**: Jisu Nam, Soowon Son, Zhan Xu, Jing Shi, Difan Liu, Feng Liu, Aashish Misraa, Seungryong Kim, Yang Zhou
- **Abstract**: We introduce Visual Persona, a foundation model for text-to-image full-body human customization that, given a single in-the-wild human image, generates diverse images of the individual guided by text descriptions. Unlike prior methods that focus solely on preserving facial identity, our approach captures detailed full-body appearance, aligning with text descriptions for body structure and scene variations. Training this model requires large-scale paired human data, consisting of multiple images per individual with consistent full-body identities, which is notoriously difficult to obtain. To address this, we propose a data curation pipeline leveraging vision-language models to evaluate full-body appearance consistency, resulting in Visual Persona-500K, a dataset of 580k paired human images across 100k unique identities. For precise appearance transfer, we introduce a transformer encoder-decoder architecture adapted to a pre-trained text-to-image diffusion model, which augments the input image into distinct body regions, encodes these regions as local appearance features, and projects them into dense identity embeddings independently to condition the diffusion model for synthesizing customized images. Visual Persona consistently surpasses existing approaches, generating high-quality, customized images from in-the-wild inputs. Extensive ablation studies validate design choices, and we demonstrate the versatility of Visual Persona across various downstream tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "Visual Persona: Foundation Model for Full-Body Human Customization" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Visual Persona," a foundation model designed for text-to-image generation focused on full-body human customization. Given a single in-the-wild human image, the model generates diverse images of the individual, guided by text descriptions, while preserving their full-body appearance.  A key challenge the paper addresses is the lack of large-scale paired human image data (multiple images of the same person).  To overcome this, the authors create Visual Persona-500K, a dataset of 580k paired human images spanning 100k unique identities, using a novel data curation pipeline leveraging vision-language models (VLMs) to ensure full-body appearance consistency.  The model architecture uses a transformer encoder-decoder adapted to a pre-trained text-to-image diffusion model. It augments the input image into distinct body regions, encodes these regions as local appearance features, and projects them into dense identity embeddings to condition the diffusion model.  Experiments demonstrate that Visual Persona surpasses existing approaches in generating high-quality, customized images from in-the-wild inputs across various downstream tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper has several novel aspects:

    *   **Full-Body Focus:**  It significantly broadens the scope from primarily face-centric human customization to full-body customization, a relatively underexplored area. This is a positive step.
    *   **Data Curation Pipeline:** The core novelty lies in the automated data curation pipeline using VLMs to create a large-scale paired human image dataset (Visual Persona-500K). The automated curation process is a significant contribution, as manually creating such datasets is extremely time-consuming and expensive. However, the reliance on LLAVA for curation introduces potential biases and inaccuracies that need careful consideration.
    *   **Architecture:** The proposed transformer encoder-decoder architecture, adapted for full-body customization, is a worthwhile contribution. The decomposition of the human image into body regions, local feature extraction, and projection into dense identity embeddings are technically interesting and seem effective.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Impact on Human Customization:** The research contributes to the growing field of human customization in generative AI. By addressing full-body appearance, it opens up applications in areas such as virtual try-on, character design, and potentially personalized content creation.
    *   **Dataset Contribution:** Visual Persona-500K provides a valuable resource for future research on human-centric generative models. Its scale surpasses that of many existing datasets, fostering more robust model development.
    *   **Methodological Advances:** The data curation pipeline provides a valuable blueprint for creating similar large-scale datasets in other domains, and the architectural design choices might influence future model designs for personalized generative tasks.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The authors provide extensive experiments, including both quantitative and qualitative results. The use of GPT-based evaluation alongside human studies strengthens the validity of their findings.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies effectively validate the contribution of each component of the proposed architecture and the influence of the data curation choices.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is generally well-written and provides sufficient detail to understand the methodology and results.
    *   **Versatility:** Demonstrating the model's application in various downstream tasks proves its flexibility and usefulness.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Pre-trained Models:** The approach relies heavily on pre-trained models (SDXL, DINov2, VLMs). While this enables strong performance, it also inherits their limitations and potential biases. The paper acknowledges some of these limitations but could delve deeper.
    *   **Potential Biases in Dataset:** While automated, the data curation process introduces potential biases through LLAVA and the face recognition model. The paper presents the demographics of the dataset but could offer a more detailed analysis of the potential impact of these biases on the generated images.
    *   **Limited Comparison:** The comparisons are limited to models that are close enough and do not include a thorough sweep of general text to image generation tasks.
    *   **Limited Analysis of Failure Cases:** A more in-depth analysis of the model's failure cases would be helpful for understanding its limitations and guiding future improvements. For example, more analysis and discussion should be given to inaccurate body proportions.
    *   **Lack of Code:** The code is not shared which limits future researchers to build upon the result.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in full-body human customization, personalized generative models, and the development of automated data curation techniques. The proposed dataset and architecture can serve as a valuable starting point for other researchers in this area.

**Score:** 7.5/10

**Justification:** The paper presents a notable contribution to the field of human customization in generative AI. The focus on full-body appearance, the innovative data curation pipeline, and the effective architecture design demonstrate significant advancements. The large dataset will be very helpful to future works. The model is not trained for multi-person but somehow does a great job.  The weaknesses primarily revolve around the reliance on pre-trained models and limited comparison, potential biases in the dataset, limited failure case analysis, and lack of code. Therefore, while the paper represents a valuable step forward, there is room for improvement in addressing these limitations and further exploring the potential of this approach.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Visual Position Prompt for MLLM based Visual Grounding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15426v1)
- **Authors**: Wei Tang, Yanpeng Sun, Qinying Gu, Zechao Li
- **Abstract**: Although Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) excel at various image-related tasks, they encounter challenges in precisely aligning coordinates with spatial information within images, particularly in position-aware tasks such as visual grounding. This limitation arises from two key factors. First, MLLMs lack explicit spatial references, making it difficult to associate textual descriptions with precise image locations. Second, their feature extraction processes prioritize global context over fine-grained spatial details, leading to weak localization capability. To address this issue, we introduce VPP-LLaVA, an MLLM equipped with Visual Position Prompt (VPP) to improve its grounding capability. VPP-LLaVA integrates two complementary mechanisms. The global VPP overlays learnable, axis-like embeddings onto the input image to provide structured spatial cues. The local VPP focuses on fine-grained localization by incorporating position-aware queries, which suggests probable object locations. We also introduce a VPP-SFT dataset with 0.6M samples, consolidating high-quality visual grounding data into a compact format for efficient model training. Training on this dataset with VPP enhances the model's performance, achieving state-of-the-art results on standard grounding benchmarks despite using fewer training samples compared to other MLLMs like MiniGPT-v2, which rely on much larger datasets ($\sim$21M samples). The code and VPP-SFT dataset will be available at https://github.com/WayneTomas/VPP-LLaVA upon acceptance.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary of the paper and conduct a critical evaluation, including a novelty/significance score.

**Summary**

The paper "Visual Position Prompt for MLLM based Visual Grounding" addresses the challenge of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) struggling with precise spatial reasoning in visual grounding tasks. The authors propose VPP-LLaVA, an MLLM augmented with Visual Position Prompts (VPPs). VPP-LLaVA employs two complementary mechanisms: a global VPP that overlays axis-like embeddings onto the image for structured spatial cues and a local VPP that incorporates position-aware queries to suggest probable object locations.  The authors also introduce a VPP-SFT dataset (0.6M samples) to consolidate high-quality visual grounding data for training. Experiments show that VPP-LLaVA achieves state-of-the-art results on grounding benchmarks with fewer training samples than other MLLMs, demonstrating improved spatial alignment and object localization. The code and VPP-SFT dataset will be made available.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies a specific weakness in MLLMs: the difficulty in precisely aligning coordinates with spatial information for visual grounding. This problem is well-motivated.
    *   **Novel Approach:** The proposed VPP-LLaVA with its global and local VPPs introduces a novel approach to explicitly inject spatial information into the MLLM. The idea of using prompts to guide the spatial reasoning of MLLMs is promising.
    *   **Well-Designed Components:** The global and local VPP components are designed in a complementary manner, addressing both overall spatial structure and object-specific localization. This design is well-reasoned.
    *   **Data Efficiency:** A significant strength is the model's ability to achieve state-of-the-art performance with a relatively small training dataset (0.6M) compared to other MLLMs (e.g., MiniGPT-v2 with 21M). This highlights the effectiveness of the proposed prompts.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of VPP-LLaVA, achieving state-of-the-art or competitive performance on several visual grounding benchmarks. The ablation studies provide further insights into the contribution of each component.
    *   **Transferability:** Transferability studies showcase the potential of VPPs, with models like VPP-LLaVA-7B demonstrating significant improvement in zero-shot visual grounding performance on the ReferIt dataset, and VPPs enabling improvements when transferred to LLaVA-NeXT-7B.
    *   **Qualitative Analysis:** Qualitative examples are included, providing a visual understanding of the model's capabilities and limitations.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on DETR:** The local VPP relies on DETR for generating object position embeddings. This makes the method somewhat dependent on the performance of the underlying object detection model. While DETR is a reasonable choice, exploring other object detection models or region proposal mechanisms might further improve performance.
    *   **Limited Ablation Studies:** Although the ablation studies are useful, some aspects could be explored further.  For example, comparing against even simpler baselines that *only* fine-tune a vanilla MLLM with the new dataset, *without* the additional prompts, would further isolate the effect of the prompts.
    *   **Dataset Composition Details:** While the paper mentions the composition of the VPP-SFT dataset, more detailed statistics on the dataset (e.g., class distribution, query length distribution) would be beneficial.
    *   **Error Case Analysis:** While failure cases are discussed, more insights could be provided on why the model struggles with certain types of queries (e.g., complex relational reasoning, ambiguous queries).
    *   **Generalization Capabilities for Caption Generation:** Limited caption generation capabilities were demonstrated compared to leading models, as indicated by the inferior METEOR score.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The explicit injection of spatial information into MLLMs using visual position prompts is a novel idea. This addresses a key limitation in existing MLLMs.
    *   The VPP-SFT dataset contributes a valuable resource for training and evaluating visual grounding models. The dataset's emphasis on a compact format is a positive aspect.
    *   The improved performance and data efficiency compared to other MLLMs demonstrate the significance of the proposed approach.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a well-motivated approach with solid experimental results. The combination of global and local visual position prompts contributes a useful methodology, especially given its ability to achieve compelling results with limited data. While the reliance on DETR, the limited scope of ablation studies, and potential enhancements regarding error-case analyses or captioning capabilities somewhat temper the overall impression, the explicit incorporation of spatial reasoning and data efficiency are compelling. Given the novelty and performance gains, this paper represents a clear step forward in visual grounding with MLLMs.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### MotionStreamer: Streaming Motion Generation via Diffusion-based Autoregressive Model in Causal Latent Space
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15451v1)
- **Authors**: Lixing Xiao, Shunlin Lu, Huaijin Pi, Ke Fan, Liang Pan, Yueer Zhou, Ziyong Feng, Xiaowei Zhou, Sida Peng, Jingbo Wang
- **Abstract**: This paper addresses the challenge of text-conditioned streaming motion generation, which requires us to predict the next-step human pose based on variable-length historical motions and incoming texts. Existing methods struggle to achieve streaming motion generation, e.g., diffusion models are constrained by pre-defined motion lengths, while GPT-based methods suffer from delayed response and error accumulation problem due to discretized non-causal tokenization. To solve these problems, we propose MotionStreamer, a novel framework that incorporates a continuous causal latent space into a probabilistic autoregressive model. The continuous latents mitigate information loss caused by discretization and effectively reduce error accumulation during long-term autoregressive generation. In addition, by establishing temporal causal dependencies between current and historical motion latents, our model fully utilizes the available information to achieve accurate online motion decoding. Experiments show that our method outperforms existing approaches while offering more applications, including multi-round generation, long-term generation, and dynamic motion composition. Project Page: https://zju3dv.github.io/MotionStreamer/
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the "MotionStreamer: Streaming Motion Generation via Diffusion-based Autoregressive Model in Causal Latent Space" paper.

**Summary**

The paper introduces MotionStreamer, a novel framework for generating human motion in a streaming fashion conditioned on text input. This means the model can generate motion in real-time, responding to text prompts as they arrive sequentially. The key innovations are: (1) using a diffusion-based autoregressive model to directly predict continuous motion latents, avoiding the need for discrete tokenization and the associated information loss; (2) a Causal Temporal AutoEncoder (Causal TAE) for compressing motion sequences into a causal latent space, enabling online decoding; and (3) two training strategies, Two-Forward training and Mixed training, to address error accumulation during long-term autoregressive generation.  The paper demonstrates state-of-the-art results on text-to-motion and long-term motion synthesis benchmarks and showcases applications like multi-round generation, long-term generation, and dynamic motion composition.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   The combination of a diffusion-based model with an autoregressive architecture for *streaming* motion generation is fairly novel. While autoregressive methods exist, their integration with diffusion and the focus on a causal latent space specifically designed for streaming are differentiating factors.
    *   The Causal TAE is another novel component.  While TAEs exist for motion compression, the emphasis on causality and continuous latents (instead of discrete tokens used by VQ-VAE variants) is a significant contribution that directly addresses the challenges of streaming generation.
    *   The Two-Forward and Mixed training strategies appear to be effective techniques for mitigating error accumulation in the autoregressive setting. While similar strategies might exist in other domains, their adaptation and application to the motion generation task are novel and practically valuable.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The ability to generate motion in a streaming manner opens up new possibilities for interactive applications, such as virtual avatars, real-time animation, and robotics. This is a significant step beyond traditional offline motion generation methods.
    *   The paper's superior performance on benchmark datasets demonstrates the effectiveness of the proposed approach, suggesting that it could become a foundation for future research in this area.
    *   The MotionStreamer framework is relatively generic and could potentially be extended to other types of sequential data generation tasks beyond motion.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of streaming motion generation and how existing methods fall short.
    *   **Well-Designed Architecture:** The components of MotionStreamer (diffusion-based autoregressive model, Causal TAE, training strategies) are well-motivated and designed to address specific problems.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper provides comprehensive experimental results on benchmark datasets and demonstrates the effectiveness of the proposed method.
    *   **Practical Applications:** The paper showcases the practical applications of MotionStreamer, further highlighting its significance.
    *   **Thorough Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies are insightful and demonstrate the importance of each component of the proposed framework.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Complexity:** The paper does not explicitly discuss the computational complexity of MotionStreamer, which could be a concern given the use of diffusion models and autoregressive architectures. Real-time applications often have strict latency requirements, so a detailed analysis of the model's efficiency is crucial.  Specifically, I would like to see benchmarks and/or more detail on runtime on consumer-grade hardware.

    *   **Generalization to Unseen Text/Motion:** Although the paper reports good performance on benchmark datasets, it's difficult to assess the model's ability to generalize to unseen text descriptions or motion styles. Future work could explore techniques for improving the model's robustness and adaptability.
    *   **Qualitative Evaluation:** While the paper includes qualitative results, a more detailed analysis of the generated motions' realism and naturalness would be beneficial. User studies could be conducted to evaluate the subjective quality of the generated motions. The qualitative comparisons could be more convincing.

    *   **Lack of Code and Data:** Although a "Project Page" is referenced, it's important to note whether or not this includes code, data, or pre-trained models for reproducibility. At the time of writing, the "Project Page" only displays the manuscript.

**Justification for Score**

Considering the novelty of the approach, the significance of the problem, the strong experimental results, and the clearly articulated architecture, but also taking into account the identified weaknesses, I would assign this paper a score of **8**.

The paper makes a strong contribution to the field of motion generation by addressing the challenging problem of streaming generation. The Causal TAE and the Two-Forward training strategies are particularly novel and effective components. However, the computational complexity and generalization ability of the model need to be further investigated. Greater detail around the qualitative evaluation of the results is also important. While the paper is well-written and presents a compelling solution, addressing these shortcomings would further enhance its impact.
Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Di$\mathtt{[M]}$O: Distilling Masked Diffusion Models into One-step Generator
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15457v1)
- **Authors**: Yuanzhi Zhu, Xi Wang, Stéphane Lathuilière, Vicky Kalogeiton
- **Abstract**: Masked Diffusion Models (MDMs) have emerged as a powerful generative modeling technique. Despite their remarkable results, they typically suffer from slow inference with several steps. In this paper, we propose Di$\mathtt{[M]}$O, a novel approach that distills masked diffusion models into a one-step generator. Di$\mathtt{[M]}$O addresses two key challenges: (1) the intractability of using intermediate-step information for one-step generation, which we solve through token-level distribution matching that optimizes model output logits by an 'on-policy framework' with the help of an auxiliary model; and (2) the lack of entropy in the initial distribution, which we address through a token initialization strategy that injects randomness while maintaining similarity to teacher training distribution. We show Di$\mathtt{[M]}$O's effectiveness on both class-conditional and text-conditional image generation, impressively achieving performance competitive to multi-step teacher outputs while drastically reducing inference time. To our knowledge, we are the first to successfully achieve one-step distillation of masked diffusion models and the first to apply discrete distillation to text-to-image generation, opening new paths for efficient generative modeling.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Di[M]O: Distilling Masked Diffusion Models into One-step Generator":

**Summary:**

The paper proposes Di[M]O, a novel method for distilling Masked Diffusion Models (MDMs) into one-step generators. Recognizing the challenges in distilling MDMs due to the inaccessibility of intermediate step information and the lack of entropy in the initial distribution, Di[M]O addresses these issues through token-level distribution matching with an auxiliary model and a token initialization strategy that injects randomness. The paper demonstrates Di[M]O's effectiveness on both class-conditional and text-conditional image generation tasks, achieving performance competitive with multi-step teacher models while significantly reducing inference time.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary contribution lies in being the first successful attempt at one-step distillation for masked diffusion models, particularly in the text-to-image domain. While distillation techniques are well-established for continuous diffusion models, the adaptation to MDMs is non-trivial due to their discrete nature and the absence of a Probability Flow ODE. The proposed token-level distribution matching strategy, combined with an auxiliary model for gradient approximation, is a novel solution to the intractability of using intermediate step information. Also novel is the approach to inject randomness into the MDM's initial state via a hybrid token initialization strategy that maintains training distribution consistency. This has been shown to mitigate mode collapse.

*   **Significance:** The ability to distill MDMs into one-step generators has significant implications for the practical use of MDMs. The drastic reduction in inference time unlocks possibilities for real-time applications and reduces computational costs, potentially making generative modeling more accessible. The paper's success in applying discrete distillation to text-to-image generation opens new research avenues for efficient generative modeling.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Addresses a challenging and relevant problem in the field of generative modeling.
    *   Presents a technically sound and well-motivated approach.
    *   Demonstrates empirical results supporting the effectiveness of Di[M]O on multiple tasks.
    *   Offers thorough ablation studies analyzing design choices.
    *   Provides clear explanations and visualizations of the proposed techniques.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper's scope is limited to specific MDM architectures (MaskGit, Meissonic). While a solid starting point, demonstrating generalizability to other MDM variants would further strengthen the results.
    *   The gains in performance are sometimes incremental relative to the computational savings. The auxiliary model adds to the complexity and training cost. The paper could more explicitly discuss trade-offs.
    *   The paper admits the failure cases of certain colors and mode collapse. Addressing the robustness of the distillation in future work would make this method have greater practical applicability.
    *   The method is data-free, and could be boosted through using real data.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to spur further research into distillation techniques for MDMs, potentially leading to more efficient and scalable generative models. The proposed token-level distribution matching strategy could also be adapted for other discrete generative models. The approach provides a critical first step that can potentially encourage more experimentation and development on this topic.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper offers a solid and novel contribution by successfully achieving one-step distillation for MDMs, a problem that has not been previously addressed effectively. The proposed techniques, token-level distribution matching and token initialization, are well-motivated and supported by empirical results. The paper effectively demonstrates the potential for significant inference speedups without a severe compromise in quality. However, the limitations in scope, the incremental gains in performance and a need for greater robustness warrant a slightly lower score. There is significant room for improvement, and the findings are applicable for a large portion of MDM usecases.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### CAM-Seg: A Continuous-valued Embedding Approach for Semantic Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15617v1)
- **Authors**: Masud Ahmed, Zahid Hasan, Syed Arefinul Haque, Abu Zaher Md Faridee, Sanjay Purushotham, Suya You, Nirmalya Roy
- **Abstract**: Traditional transformer-based semantic segmentation relies on quantized embeddings. However, our analysis reveals that autoencoder accuracy on segmentation mask using quantized embeddings (e.g. VQ-VAE) is 8% lower than continuous-valued embeddings (e.g. KL-VAE). Motivated by this, we propose a continuous-valued embedding framework for semantic segmentation. By reformulating semantic mask generation as a continuous image-to-embedding diffusion process, our approach eliminates the need for discrete latent representations while preserving fine-grained spatial and semantic details. Our key contribution includes a diffusion-guided autoregressive transformer that learns a continuous semantic embedding space by modeling long-range dependencies in image features. Our framework contains a unified architecture combining a VAE encoder for continuous feature extraction, a diffusion-guided transformer for conditioned embedding generation, and a VAE decoder for semantic mask reconstruction. Our setting facilitates zero-shot domain adaptation capabilities enabled by the continuity of the embedding space. Experiments across diverse datasets (e.g., Cityscapes and domain-shifted variants) demonstrate state-of-the-art robustness to distribution shifts, including adverse weather (e.g., fog, snow) and viewpoint variations. Our model also exhibits strong noise resilience, achieving robust performance ($\approx$ 95% AP compared to baseline) under gaussian noise, moderate motion blur, and moderate brightness/contrast variations, while experiencing only a moderate impact ($\approx$ 90% AP compared to baseline) from 50% salt and pepper noise, saturation and hue shifts. Code available: https://github.com/mahmed10/CAMSS.git
- **Summary**: Okay, I've reviewed the paper "CAM-Seg: A Continuous-valued Embedding Approach for Semantic Image Generation". Here's a summary and critical evaluation.

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel approach to semantic image segmentation called CAM-Seg, which uses continuous-valued embeddings instead of the more common quantized embeddings. It argues that quantized embeddings, while simpler to handle due to their compatibility with standard categorical distributions, lose fine-grained spatial and semantic details. CAM-Seg uses a VAE to extract continuous-valued embeddings from input RGB images and their corresponding semantic masks. A diffusion-guided autoregressive transformer then learns a continuous semantic embedding space.  The diffusion model computes the loss for backpropagation during training and facilitates sampling for semantic embedding generation during inference, which are decoded to semantic segmentation outputs.  The method demonstrates state-of-the-art robustness to domain shifts (e.g., adverse weather) and noise, and exhibits zero-shot domain adaptation capabilities. The authors perform extensive experiments on datasets like Cityscapes, SemanticKITTI, ACDC, and CADEdgeTune.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The main novelty lies in the combination of several existing techniques into a new framework for semantic segmentation. The idea of using continuous-valued embeddings is not entirely new (as the paper itself acknowledges), nor is the use of diffusion models or autoregressive transformers. However, the specific *combination* of a VAE encoder for continuous feature extraction, a diffusion-guided transformer for conditioned embedding generation, and a VAE decoder for semantic mask reconstruction, *specifically tailored* for semantic segmentation, seems to be a unique contribution.  The integration of bidirectional attention (inspired by MAE) into the transformer for enhancing feature learning is another incremental but potentially impactful addition.

**Significance:**

*   **Performance:** The paper demonstrates strong empirical results, particularly in terms of robustness to noise, domain shifts, and zero-shot adaptation. Achieving state-of-the-art results in these areas is significant, especially given the increasing importance of deploying segmentation models in real-world, unpredictable environments.
*   **Continuous Embeddings:** The paper makes a convincing argument for the benefits of continuous-valued embeddings over quantized ones for this task. Quantized embeddings can lead to loss of information during discretization.
*   **Unified Architecture:** The development of a unified architecture is a positive aspect, facilitating zero-shot domain adaptation.
*   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies are comprehensive and provide useful insights into the impact of different design choices, like input image size and semantic image color mapping.

**Weaknesses and Limitations:**

*   **Incremental Nature:** While the combination of techniques is novel, the individual components are well-established. The core innovation is primarily architectural, not algorithmic.
*   **Computational Cost:** The paper does not provide a detailed analysis of the computational cost of CAM-Seg compared to other methods. Diffusion models, in particular, can be computationally expensive.
*   **Dark Environments:** The model's struggle in extremely dark environments is a notable limitation. Many real-world applications of semantic segmentation involve low-light conditions.
*   **Lack of Benchmark Comparison:** The authors admit the lack of directly comparable approaches, preventing a rigorous benchmarking against established methods, although the authors show solid overall results, this limits the significance and impact of the paper.
*   **Limited novelty of the results:** The paper leverages a lot of current technology and is able to extract some advantages, but the performance gains aren't high enough to create a very large impact.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper could influence future research in semantic segmentation by highlighting the benefits of continuous-valued embeddings and providing a concrete framework for their use.  The emphasis on robustness and zero-shot adaptation is also timely and relevant. Other researchers can build upon this work by exploring different architectures, loss functions, or training strategies for continuous-valued semantic segmentation.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses, I'm assigning a score of **6.5**. This score reflects the paper's solid empirical results, novel architectural combination, and potential impact on the field, *balanced* by the incremental nature of the core ideas and some limitations in computational efficiency and low-light robustness. The limited benchmarking, coupled with the relatively marginal gains compared to existing methods, reduces its impact relative to a truly breakthrough work.

Score: 6.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### LLaVA-MORE: A Comparative Study of LLMs and Visual Backbones for Enhanced Visual Instruction Tuning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15621v1)
- **Authors**: Federico Cocchi, Nicholas Moratelli, Davide Caffagni, Sara Sarto, Lorenzo Baraldi, Marcella Cornia, Rita Cucchiara
- **Abstract**: Recent progress in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) has highlighted the critical roles of both the visual backbone and the underlying language model. While prior work has primarily focused on scaling these components to billions of parameters, the trade-offs between model size, architecture, and performance remain underexplored. Additionally, inconsistencies in training data and evaluation protocols have hindered direct comparisons, making it difficult to derive optimal design choices. In this paper, we introduce LLaVA-MORE, a new family of MLLMs that integrates recent language models with diverse visual backbones. To ensure fair comparisons, we employ a unified training protocol applied consistently across all architectures. Our analysis systematically explores both small- and medium-scale LLMs -- including Phi-4, LLaMA-3.1, and Gemma-2 -- to evaluate multimodal reasoning, generation, and instruction following, while examining the relationship between model size and performance. Beyond evaluating the LLM impact on final results, we conduct a comprehensive study of various visual encoders, ranging from CLIP-based architectures to alternatives such as DINOv2, SigLIP, and SigLIP2. Additional experiments investigate the effects of increased image resolution and variations in pre-training datasets. Overall, our results provide insights into the design of more effective MLLMs, offering a reproducible evaluation framework that facilitates direct comparisons and can guide future model development. Our source code and trained models are publicly available at: https://github.com/aimagelab/LLaVA-MORE.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "LLaVA-MORE: A Comparative Study of LLMs and Visual Backbones for Enhanced Visual Instruction Tuning" introduces a new family of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) called LLaVA-MORE. The core contribution is a systematic exploration of different LLM backbones (Phi-4, LLaMA-3, Gemma-2) and visual encoders (CLIP, DINOv2, SigLIP, SigLIP2) within the LLaVA framework. The authors emphasize a unified training protocol to ensure fair comparisons across various architectures.  They investigate the trade-offs between model size, architecture, pre-training datasets, and image resolution, aiming to provide insights for designing more effective MLLMs.  The paper also makes its code and trained models publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Systematic Study:** The paper provides a valuable systematic comparison of different LLMs and visual backbones. Such comprehensive studies are crucial for advancing the field beyond simply scaling up existing models.
    *   **Unified Training Protocol:** The emphasis on a unified training protocol is a significant strength, addressing a common problem in MLLM research where inconsistent training procedures make direct comparisons difficult.  This significantly increases the reliability and interpretability of the results.
    *   **Open Source:** Releasing the code and model checkpoints promotes reproducibility and facilitates further research by the community.
    *   **Analysis of Multiple Factors:** The paper delves into the effects of various factors (model size, visual backbone, pre-training data, image resolution) on MLLM performance, providing a nuanced understanding of their interactions.
    *   **Interesting findings**: The paper includes several key findings, such as the effectiveness of image-text pre-trained visual backbones, the diminishing returns of increased resolution as the model scales up, and the varying impact of pre-training data sources. They also highlighted how several small models performed in comparison to larger models of the previous generation.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the systematic study is valuable, the individual components (LLMs and visual backbones) are not entirely novel. The architecture itself largely follows the established LLaVA framework. The novelty lies primarily in the combination and the comparative analysis.
    *   **Limited Scope of Investigation:** While multiple architectures are compared, the scope might not cover the very latest advancements in both LLMs and vision transformers. It would have been good to include models based on Mixtral or more recent LLMs.
    *   **Benchmark Choice:** The reliance on standard benchmarks, while necessary for comparison, might not fully capture the complexity of real-world multimodal tasks.  More specialized or challenging benchmarks could provide further insights.
    *   **Lack of In-depth Analysis of Failure Cases:** The paper focuses primarily on overall performance metrics. A deeper analysis of specific failure cases (e.g., types of questions or images where the models struggle) would be more insightful.
    *   **Limited Ablation Studies**: The paper mostly compares different model configurations, rather than conducting focused ablation studies to isolate the impact of specific design choices (e.g., adapter architecture, training objectives).

*   **Significance:**

    The paper has moderate significance for the MLLM field. Its systematic approach helps to clarify the trade-offs involved in MLLM design. The publicly available code and models will facilitate further research. However, the incremental nature of its novelty limits its overall impact.

*   **Potential Influence:**
    The paper's main influence is likely to be in providing a solid baseline and framework for future MLLM development. Other researchers can build upon the comparative analysis and use the released resources to experiment with new architectures and training strategies. It also highlights the importance of robust evaluation protocols for the MLLM community.

**Score: 6**

**Justification:**

The paper warrants a score of 6. While the systematic study and unified training protocol are commendable, the limited architectural novelty and reliance on existing components (LLMs and visual backbones) prevent it from achieving a higher score. The publicly available resources enhance its practical value, but the impact is primarily incremental rather than revolutionary. A deeper analysis of failure cases and targeted ablation studies could have significantly strengthened the paper's conclusions and increased its impact. The contributions are solid and valuable to the community but lack the groundbreaking nature to warrant a significantly higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### R$^2$: A LLM Based Novel-to-Screenplay Generation Framework with Causal Plot Graphs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15655v1)
- **Authors**: Zefeng Lin, Yi Xiao, Zhiqiang Mo, Qifan Zhang, Jie Wang, Jiayang Chen, Jiajing Zhang, Hui Zhang, Zhengyi Liu, Xianyong Fang, Xiaohua Xu
- **Abstract**: Automatically adapting novels into screenplays is important for the TV, film, or opera industries to promote products with low costs. The strong performances of large language models (LLMs) in long-text generation call us to propose a LLM based framework Reader-Rewriter (R$^2$) for this task. However, there are two fundamental challenges here. First, the LLM hallucinations may cause inconsistent plot extraction and screenplay generation. Second, the causality-embedded plot lines should be effectively extracted for coherent rewriting. Therefore, two corresponding tactics are proposed: 1) A hallucination-aware refinement method (HAR) to iteratively discover and eliminate the affections of hallucinations; and 2) a causal plot-graph construction method (CPC) based on a greedy cycle-breaking algorithm to efficiently construct plot lines with event causalities. Recruiting those efficient techniques, R$^2$ utilizes two modules to mimic the human screenplay rewriting process: The Reader module adopts a sliding window and CPC to build the causal plot graphs, while the Rewriter module generates first the scene outlines based on the graphs and then the screenplays. HAR is integrated into both modules for accurate inferences of LLMs. Experimental results demonstrate the superiority of R$^2$, which substantially outperforms three existing approaches (51.3%, 22.6%, and 57.1% absolute increases) in pairwise comparison at the overall win rate for GPT-4o.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces R², a novel LLM-based framework for automatically adapting novels into screenplays. It addresses two key challenges: 1) LLM hallucinations leading to inconsistent plot extraction and screenplay generation, and 2) effectively extracting causality-embedded plot lines for coherent rewriting.  The R² framework consists of two main modules: a Reader (which extracts events and character profiles) and a Rewriter (which generates scene outlines and screenplays). Two primary techniques are proposed: (1) a Hallucination-Aware Refinement (HAR) method to iteratively reduce inconsistencies, and (2) a Causal Plot-Graph Construction (CPC) method to build plot lines with event causalities. The Reader employs a sliding window technique and CPC, while the Rewriter generates scene outlines and then screenplays.  Experimental results demonstrate the superiority of R² over existing approaches based on both GPT-4 and human evaluation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper tackles an interesting and practically relevant problem: automatic novel-to-screenplay generation. The R² framework, with its Reader-Rewriter architecture and the HAR and CPC components, represents a reasonable and well-structured approach to address the identified challenges.

*   **Novelty:** The specific combination of techniques (HAR and CPC within a Reader-Rewriter framework for N2SG) appears to be novel. While individual components (LLM-based generation, iterative refinement, plot graph extraction) have been explored in other contexts, their integration here for this specific task seems unique. The sliding window approach in the Reader module and the cycle-breaking algorithm within CPC also seem like well-motivated contributions.

*   **Significance:** The problem of automating screenplay adaptation is significant due to its potential to reduce production costs and promote content dissemination. Demonstrating a substantial improvement in the quality of automatically generated screenplays could have a notable impact on the TV, film, and opera industries. The performance gains reported (significant increases in win rates compared to baselines) suggest a practical improvement.  The use of both automated (GPT-4) and human evaluation strengthens the claims.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clearly defines the problem and challenges.
    *   Proposes a well-structured framework with clear modules and techniques.
    *   Provides a good level of detail regarding the HAR and CPC methods.
    *   Presents quantitative results with both automated and human evaluation, demonstrating improvements over baselines.
    *   Includes an ablation study to assess the contribution of different components.
    *   Qualitative case study illustrating the strengths of the approach.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The technical details of the HAR and CPC methods could be further elaborated.  For example, the specific prompts used for hallucination detection and refinement, as well as the criteria for selecting edges to remove in the greedy cycle-breaking algorithm, warrant further clarification.
    *   The dataset used for evaluation is not readily available (needs manual cleaning of public data). This hinders reproducibility and independent verification of the results. While the authors claim the dataset will be made available, the assessment here must consider the absence of such availability.
    *   The evaluation, while comprehensive, relies on relatively short excerpts (1000 tokens). It's unclear how well the framework scales to full-length novels and screenplays.
    *   GPT-4 is used as an evaluator, this is acceptable, it's important to be aware of potential biases of GPT-4 towards certain writing styles, content or other aspects.
    *   There appears to be an improvement in Coherency between Dramatron and the baseline. The paper should mention that this might be a factor as to why the new approach might not see an increase in Coherency.

*   **Potential Influence:** If the framework proves to be robust and scalable, it could influence the development of future tools for automated screenplay generation and potentially be adopted by content creators. The HAR and CPC techniques might also be applicable in other long-form text generation tasks.

Overall, the paper makes a solid contribution to the field. The R² framework and the proposed techniques address relevant challenges in novel-to-screenplay generation and the experimental results suggest practical improvements over existing methods.  However, the limited dataset availability, lack of detail on the methods, and excerpt-based evaluation temper the assessment of its impact.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Enhancing Pancreatic Cancer Staging with Large Language Models: The Role of Retrieval-Augmented Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15664v1)
- **Authors**: Hisashi Johno, Yuki Johno, Akitomo Amakawa, Junichi Sato, Ryota Tozuka, Atsushi Komaba, Hiroaki Watanabe, Hiroki Watanabe, Chihiro Goto, Hiroyuki Morisaka, Hiroshi Onishi, Kazunori Nakamoto
- **Abstract**: Purpose: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) is a technology to enhance the functionality and reliability of large language models (LLMs) by retrieving relevant information from reliable external knowledge (REK). RAG has gained interest in radiology, and we previously reported the utility of NotebookLM, an LLM with RAG (RAG-LLM), for lung cancer staging. However, since the comparator LLM differed from NotebookLM's internal model, it remained unclear whether its advantage stemmed from RAG or inherent model differences. To better isolate RAG's impact and assess its utility across different cancers, we compared NotebookLM with its internal LLM, Gemini 2.0 Flash, in a pancreatic cancer staging experiment. Materials and Methods: A summary of Japan's pancreatic cancer staging guidelines was used as REK. We compared three groups - REK+/RAG+ (NotebookLM with REK), REK+/RAG- (Gemini 2.0 Flash with REK), and REK-/RAG- (Gemini 2.0 Flash without REK) - in staging 100 fictional pancreatic cancer cases based on CT findings. Staging criteria included TNM classification, local invasion factors, and resectability classification. In REK+/RAG+, retrieval accuracy was quantified based on the sufficiency of retrieved REK excerpts. Results: REK+/RAG+ achieved a staging accuracy of 70%, outperforming REK+/RAG- (38%) and REK-/RAG- (35%). For TNM classification, REK+/RAG+ attained 80% accuracy, exceeding REK+/RAG- (55%) and REK-/RAG- (50%). Additionally, REK+/RAG+ explicitly presented retrieved REK excerpts, achieving a retrieval accuracy of 92%. Conclusion: NotebookLM, a RAG-LLM, outperformed its internal LLM, Gemini 2.0 Flash, in a pancreatic cancer staging experiment, suggesting that RAG may improve LLM's staging accuracy. Furthermore, its ability to retrieve and present REK excerpts provides transparency for physicians, highlighting its applicability for clinical diagnosis and classification.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

This paper investigates the use of Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) with Large Language Models (LLMs) for pancreatic cancer staging.  It compares the performance of NotebookLM (a RAG-LLM) with its internal LLM, Gemini 2.0 Flash, and Gemini 2.0 Flash without RAG in a pancreatic cancer staging task using fictional patient data. The study found that NotebookLM, leveraging RAG with external knowledge (REK), significantly outperformed Gemini 2.0 Flash, both with and without REK, in staging accuracy and in the accurate retrieval of relevant information.  The authors conclude that RAG enhances the accuracy of LLMs in pancreatic cancer staging and highlights the potential of RAG-LLMs in supporting clinical diagnosis and classification, although emphasizing the need for caution regarding medical applications and information security.

**Critical Evaluation:**
