However, because the cryptographic prompt injection (more complex) is only applicable to white-box models (and it is not clear how this could translate to black-box models) and because some of the prompt injection (font embedding) seems to be manually done, the top score is reduced to 8, with an emphasis on future work on improving practicality and robustness.

- **Classification**: cs.DL
- **Score**: 8/10

### AutoDrive-QA- Automated Generation of Multiple-Choice Questions for Autonomous Driving Datasets Using Large Vision-Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15778v1)
- **Authors**: Boshra Khalili, Andrew W. Smyth
- **Abstract**: In autonomous driving, open-ended question answering often suffers from unreliable evaluations because freeform responses require either complex metrics or subjective human judgment. To address this challenge, we introduce AutoDrive-QA, an automatic pipeline that converts existing driving QA datasets (including DriveLM, NuScenes-QA, and LingoQA) into a structured multiple-choice question (MCQ) format. This benchmark systematically assesses perception, prediction, and planning tasks, providing a standardized and objective evaluation framework. AutoDrive-QA employs an automated pipeline that leverages large language models (LLMs) to generate high-quality, contextually relevant distractors based on domain-specific error patterns commonly found in autonomous driving scenarios. To evaluate both general capabilities and generalization performance, we test the benchmark on three public datasets and conduct zero-shot experiments on an unseen dataset. The zero-shot evaluations reveal that GPT-4V leads with 69.57% accuracy -- achieving 74.94% in Perception, 65.33% in Prediction, and 68.45% in Planning -- demonstrating that while all models excel in Perception, they struggle in Prediction. Consequently, AutoDrive-QA establishes a rigorous, unbiased standard for integrating and evaluating different vision-language models across various autonomous driving datasets, thereby improving generalization in this field. We release all the codes in the AutoDrive-QA GitHub Repository.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AutoDrive-QA, an automated pipeline designed to convert existing autonomous driving question answering (QA) datasets into a multiple-choice question (MCQ) format. This aims to address issues of unreliable evaluation common in open-ended QA, which often requires complex metrics or subjective human judgment. AutoDrive-QA leverages large language models (LLMs) to generate contextually relevant and domain-specific distractors, reflecting common errors in perception, prediction, and planning scenarios. The benchmark is tested on three public datasets, and zero-shot performance is evaluated, demonstrating GPT-4V's effectiveness, particularly in perception tasks, while highlighting challenges in prediction. The key contributions are a unified driving QA benchmark, an automated MCQ generation pipeline with domain-specific error modeling, and an error analysis framework.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The core novelty lies in the automated generation of multiple-choice questions, specifically tailored to the autonomous driving domain. While automated MCQ generation is not entirely new, the paper's emphasis on generating *realistic* distractors based on common errors in autonomous driving tasks enhances its novelty. The approach of integrating multiple existing datasets into a unified benchmark is also a significant contribution, promoting a more comprehensive assessment of VLM capabilities in this area.

**Significance:**

The significance of this work stems from the need for standardized and objective evaluation metrics in the rapidly evolving field of autonomous driving. The MCQ format addresses the limitations of open-ended QA, making model performance comparison more straightforward and reliable. The domain-specific error modeling adds robustness to the benchmark, forcing models to demonstrate true understanding rather than relying on superficial cues to eliminate trivial distractors. The paper's error analysis framework further enhances its value by providing insights into the specific strengths and weaknesses of different VLM models. This allows researchers to better target their efforts in improving the capabilities of these models.

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses a critical problem:** The paper tackles a real and pressing challenge in autonomous driving research—the lack of consistent and objective evaluation metrics.
*   **Comprehensive approach:** The paper presents a complete pipeline from dataset integration to MCQ generation to error analysis.
*   **Domain-specific customization:**  The paper's emphasis on generating realistic distractors based on domain-specific knowledge adds significant value.
*   **Clear evaluation and analysis:**  The paper includes a clear evaluation of model performance and a detailed error analysis.
*   **Open Source:** The authors publicly release all the codes in AutoDrive-QA GitHub Repository

**Weaknesses:**

*   **LLM Dependency:** The quality of the generated MCQs hinges on the capabilities of the underlying LLMs.  The authors acknowledge that questions heavily rely on prompt engineering. Future LLM updates may affect the reliability of the evaluation.
*   **Limited Scope:** While the benchmark integrates three datasets, it still represents a limited scope of autonomous driving scenarios. Expanding the benchmark to include more diverse datasets and tasks could further enhance its generalizability.
*   **Zero-shot focus:**  The paper focuses on zero-shot evaluation. Finetuning LLMs may be necessary to reach production-level safety in autonomous driving.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a valuable and well-executed approach to automated MCQ generation for autonomous driving. The focus on realistic distractors and the comprehensive evaluation framework significantly contribute to the field by addressing crucial challenges in model evaluation. However, dependence on LLM performance and scope limitations justify a slightly lower score. I think future steps should include fine-tuning.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Grammar and Gameplay-aligned RL for Game Description Generation with LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15783v1)
- **Authors**: Tsunehiko Tanaka, Edgar Simo-Serra
- **Abstract**: Game Description Generation (GDG) is the task of generating a game description written in a Game Description Language (GDL) from natural language text. Previous studies have explored generation methods leveraging the contextual understanding capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs); however, accurately reproducing the game features of the game descriptions remains a challenge. In this paper, we propose reinforcement learning-based fine-tuning of LLMs for GDG (RLGDG). Our training method simultaneously improves grammatical correctness and fidelity to game concepts by introducing both grammar rewards and concept rewards. Furthermore, we adopt a two-stage training strategy where Reinforcement Learning (RL) is applied following Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT). Experimental results demonstrate that our proposed method significantly outperforms baseline methods using SFT alone.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RLGDG, a method for generating Game Description Language (GDL) code from natural language descriptions of games using reinforcement learning (RL). The approach fine-tunes Large Language Models (LLMs) using a two-stage process: first, Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT) to learn basic GDL syntax and structure, then Reinforcement Learning Fine-Tuning (RLFT) to optimize for both grammatical correctness and fidelity to the intended game mechanics.  The RLFT stage uses grammar and concept-based rewards to guide the LLM. The authors demonstrate through experiments that their method outperforms baselines that only use SFT, improving both grammatical accuracy and game concept fidelity.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonably novel approach by integrating RL into the game description generation process. While using LLMs for GDL generation isn't entirely new (as referenced by the authors), the *specific* combination of SFT followed by RLFT with grammar and concept rewards is a contribution.  The specific formulation of reward functions, particularly the conceptual reward considering game-play simulations, is a key aspect of novelty.

*   **Significance:** Generating accurate and reliable GDL is important for automated game design and AI research, making this a significant application area. The paper demonstrates a clear improvement over SFT-only baselines, suggesting that the RL approach has the potential to generate more useful and reliable GDL. The emphasis on ensuring not just syntactical correctness but also semantic fidelity is a valuable focus, making the resulting game descriptions more directly useful for game engines and AI agents. The paper tackles a practical problem and provides a solution that addresses its challenges in a meaningful way.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly defines the problem of GDG and its limitations when relying on LLMs alone.
    *   **Well-Defined Method:** The RLGDG method is well-explained, including the two-stage training process and the reward modeling.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experiments are thorough and show statistically significant improvements over baselines, covering multiple evaluation metrics (Compilability, Functionality, ROUGE-L, NCD). The ablation study also effectively highlights the contribution of each reward component (grammar and concept). Qualitative analysis provides further insights into the improvement.
    *   **Focus on Semantic Fidelity:** The paper correctly identifies and tackles the challenge of generating GDL that accurately reflects the gameplay, not just the syntax.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Dataset:**  The dataset size is relatively small.  While the authors explain their choice of using games with limited token lengths, expanding this would enhance the generalizability of the findings. They acknowledge and partially address this through game category analysis.
    *   **Model Size:** While the choice of Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct is justified by reproducibility and resource constraints, experiments with larger models could further demonstrate the potential of the approach.
    *   **Computational Cost:** The RL fine-tuning process likely has a high computational cost. It would be useful to report the training time and resource consumption to assess its practicality.
    *   **Dependence on Ludii Engine:** The reward design and evaluation metrics are tied to the Ludii GDL. While this is a valid choice, it limits the direct applicability to other game description languages.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's approach of using RL to fine-tune LLMs for structured output generation could be applicable to other domains beyond game description generation, where both syntactic correctness and semantic accuracy are important. The reward function design is particularly insightful.

*   **Missing Information:** The paper lacks the implementation details of the Earley parser and Ludii GDL grammar.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7**. The paper has good novelty in its use of RL to enhance GDL generation. It addresses a practical problem with a well-designed method and demonstrates significant improvements through thorough experiments. However, the limited dataset size, the use of a smaller-scale LLM, and the dependence on a specific game description language somewhat limit the overall impact and generalizability.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### RL4Med-DDPO: Reinforcement Learning for Controlled Guidance Towards Diverse Medical Image Generation using Vision-Language Foundation Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15784v1)
- **Authors**: Parham Saremi, Amar Kumar, Mohammed Mohammed, Zahra TehraniNasab, Tal Arbel
- **Abstract**: Vision-Language Foundation Models (VLFM) have shown a tremendous increase in performance in terms of generating high-resolution, photorealistic natural images. While VLFMs show a rich understanding of semantic content across modalities, they often struggle with fine-grained alignment tasks that require precise correspondence between image regions and textual descriptions a limitation in medical imaging, where accurate localization and detection of clinical features are essential for diagnosis and analysis. To address this issue, we propose a multi-stage architecture where a pre-trained VLFM provides a cursory semantic understanding, while a reinforcement learning (RL) algorithm refines the alignment through an iterative process that optimizes for understanding semantic context. The reward signal is designed to align the semantic information of the text with synthesized images. We demonstrate the effectiveness of our method on a medical imaging skin dataset where the generated images exhibit improved generation quality and alignment with prompt over the fine-tuned Stable Diffusion. We also show that the synthesized samples could be used to improve disease classifier performance for underrepresented subgroups through augmentation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "RL4Med-DDPO: Reinforcement Learning for Controlled Guidance Towards Diverse Medical Image Generation using Vision-Language Foundation Models" introduces a novel approach to improving medical image generation using Vision-Language Foundation Models (VLFMs). Recognizing the limitations of standard VLFMs in fine-grained alignment between text prompts and generated images, especially critical in medical imaging, the authors propose a multi-stage architecture. This involves a pre-trained VLFM for initial semantic understanding, followed by a reinforcement learning (RL) algorithm to refine the alignment. The RL algorithm uses a reward signal designed to align the semantic information of the text prompt with the synthesized images. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of their method on a skin lesion dataset, showing improved image quality, prompt alignment, and potential for augmenting datasets to improve disease classifier performance for underrepresented subgroups. The method leverages Stable Diffusion as the base VLFM and introduces a new metric, Artifact Prevalence Rate (APR), to quantify the presence of undesired artifacts in the generated images.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the application of reinforcement learning specifically to refine text-to-image generation within the *medical imaging* domain.  While RL-guided diffusion models exist in the general image generation space (e.g., DDPO), the specific adaptation to address alignment issues in medical images, along with the introduction of the APR metric, adds a degree of novelty. The idea of using RL to control attributes in generated images isn't entirely new, but its application *in this particular context and with this specific reward function design* is a contribution.

**Significance:** The significance of this work stems from its potential to address crucial challenges in medical imaging: data bias and the need for precise image generation for diagnosis and analysis. Generating high-quality, aligned synthetic data can have substantial benefits, including augmenting datasets to improve the performance of diagnostic models, especially for underrepresented patient populations. If the method consistently produces realistic and reliable synthetic data, it could lead to more robust and equitable AI-driven healthcare. Moreover, counterfactual data augmentation can assist in testing hypotheses about the causal factors of disease.

**Strengths:**

*   **Problem Relevance:** The paper addresses a clear and important problem in medical imaging: the need for accurate and aligned image generation to overcome data bias and improve diagnostic accuracy.
*   **Methodological Soundness:** The use of RL to fine-tune a pre-trained VLFM is a reasonable approach, building on existing work in the field while tailoring it to the medical imaging domain.
*   **Experimental Validation:** The experimental results, including the quantitative evaluation using APR and the qualitative comparisons, provide evidence of the method's effectiveness.
*   **Practical Application:** The paper demonstrates the potential of the generated images to improve disease classifier performance, highlighting its practical applicability.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Dataset:** The evaluation is primarily based on a single skin lesion dataset (ISIC2019). While relevant, it would be significantly strengthened by demonstrating its effectiveness on other medical imaging modalities (e.g., X-rays, MRIs) and different disease types.  Generalizability needs to be more clearly addressed.
*   **APR Metric Limitations:** The APR metric is defined based on the output of a pre-trained classifier. The accuracy of APR is therefore dependent on the performance of the said classifier. If the classifier is biased or inaccurate, the APR results can be misleading. This dependency and its potential impact need to be discussed and mitigated.
*   **Reward Function Specificity:** The reward function design seems heavily tailored to the skin lesion dataset. The transferability of this specific reward function to other medical imaging tasks might be limited, requiring careful redesign and tuning.
*   **Computational Cost:** The RL fine-tuning process can be computationally expensive, which might limit its accessibility to researchers and practitioners with limited resources. This should be discussed more explicitly.
*   **Clarity in Baseline Comparison**: While comparisons with Stable Diffusion are made, the exact fine-tuning approach of the baseline SD model should be made more explicit in the experiments section. What fine-tuning method was used and for how long?

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence the field by:

*   Encouraging further research into RL-guided medical image generation.
*   Providing a benchmark for evaluating the alignment of text prompts and generated medical images.
*   Facilitating the development of more robust and equitable AI-driven diagnostic tools.

**Score:** 6.5

**Justification:**

The paper presents a reasonably novel and relevant application of RL to a significant problem in medical image generation. The experimental results are promising, but the limited dataset and the dependence of the APR metric on the pre-trained classifier's accuracy are significant weaknesses. Furthermore, the potential lack of generalizability of the reward function design and the computational cost limit its immediate impact.  Addressing these limitations through more diverse experimental validation, a more thorough discussion of the APR metric, and exploration of more efficient RL techniques would significantly improve the paper's impact and justify a higher score. The clarity of the experiments section, particularly with regards to the finetuning of the baseline SD, should be improved. Despite these weaknesses, the core idea and initial results warrant a moderate positive score, acknowledging its potential contribution to the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Controlling Avatar Diffusion with Learnable Gaussian Embedding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15809v1)
- **Authors**: Xuan Gao, Jingtao Zhou, Dongyu Liu, Yuqi Zhou, Juyong Zhang
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in diffusion models have made significant progress in digital human generation. However, most existing models still struggle to maintain 3D consistency, temporal coherence, and motion accuracy. A key reason for these shortcomings is the limited representation ability of commonly used control signals(e.g., landmarks, depth maps, etc.). In addition, the lack of diversity in identity and pose variations in public datasets further hinders progress in this area. In this paper, we analyze the shortcomings of current control signals and introduce a novel control signal representation that is optimizable, dense, expressive, and 3D consistent. Our method embeds a learnable neural Gaussian onto a parametric head surface, which greatly enhances the consistency and expressiveness of diffusion-based head models. Regarding the dataset, we synthesize a large-scale dataset with multiple poses and identities. In addition, we use real/synthetic labels to effectively distinguish real and synthetic data, minimizing the impact of imperfections in synthetic data on the generated head images. Extensive experiments show that our model outperforms existing methods in terms of realism, expressiveness, and 3D consistency. Our code, synthetic datasets, and pre-trained models will be released in our project page: https://ustc3dv.github.io/Learn2Control/
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper introduces a novel approach to controlling avatar diffusion models, aiming to improve 3D consistency, expressiveness, and realism. The core innovations are: 1) a learnable Gaussian embedding used as a control signal, replacing traditional visual cues like landmarks or depth maps, and 2) a training strategy that leverages a large-scale synthetic dataset coupled with a real/synthetic discriminator to mitigate artifacts introduced by the synthetic data. The learnable Gaussian embedding, which is splatted from a neural Gaussian onto a parametric head surface, is designed to be dense, adaptive, and 3D-consistent. Extensive experiments demonstrate that the proposed method outperforms existing approaches in terms of realism, expressiveness, and 3D consistency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents two main novelties. The first, and arguably more significant, is the learnable Gaussian embedding as a control signal. While 3D Gaussians are not entirely new in head modeling, their use as the *sole* control signal for a diffusion model, especially in the context of avatar creation, is a distinctive contribution. It is different from existing methods that leverage Gaussian splatting for rendering and/or as primitives to *represent* the output but not as an "input" control signal. The synthetic data generation and real/synthetic discriminator strategy is more incremental. Using synthetic data for training is common, but the specific method of artifact mitigation via a discriminator adds to the overall effectiveness.

*   **Significance:** The claimed improvements in 3D consistency and expressiveness are important for the avatar creation field. The problems the paper addresses are well-recognized limitations of existing methods. The experimental results, if verifiable and robust, support the claim that the proposed approach offers a significant advantage over current techniques. The potential impact is reasonably high if the method generalizes well to diverse identities and unseen poses. The method's ability to operate without relying on visual cues, but rather adaptively learning and encoding geometric features directly, may be a considerable step forward.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novel Control Signal:** The learnable Gaussian embedding appears to be a clever way to encode dense geometric information in a 3D-consistent manner, which is crucial for accurate head modeling.
    *   **Artifact Mitigation:** The real/synthetic discriminator is a practical approach to addressing a common problem in synthetic data training.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper reports significant improvements in various metrics compared to existing methods, suggesting that the approach is effective.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependence on FLAME:** The reliance on the FLAME head model is both a strength and a weakness. It provides a good parametric base, but it also inherently limits the model's ability to generate identities that are vastly different from those captured by FLAME.
    *   **Limited Detail about Gaussians:** While the gaussian splatting is explained well, details about the neural gaussian embeddings, training and initializations of the Gaussians are glossed over.
    *   **Generalization Concerns:** The quantitative evaluation, while strong, needs to be supplemented with more qualitative results showcasing diverse examples to assess the generalization. The quality of the synthetic data will strongly influence generalization capabilities. There should be a section on the parameters, the loss functions, and training details for the Gaussians.

*   **Justification for Score:** The score is based on the overall novelty and potential impact, balanced against the limitations. The use of learnable Gaussian embeddings as a control signal for diffusion models is novel and potentially impactful. The rest of the method is incremental. While the experimental results look good, the overall improvement is somewhat limited.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.GR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Attention Pruning: Automated Fairness Repair of Language Models via Surrogate Simulated Annealing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15815v1)
- **Authors**: Vishnu Asutosh Dasu, Md Rafi ur Rashid, Vipul Gupta, Saeid Tizpaz-Niari, Gang Tan
- **Abstract**: This paper explores pruning attention heads as a post-processing bias mitigation method for large language models (LLMs). Modern AI systems such as LLMs are expanding into sensitive social contexts where fairness concerns become especially crucial. Since LLMs develop decision-making patterns by training on massive datasets of human-generated content, they naturally encode and perpetuate societal biases. While modifying training datasets and algorithms is expensive and requires significant resources; post-processing techniques-such as selectively deactivating neurons and attention heads in pre-trained LLMs-can provide feasible and effective approaches to improve fairness. However, identifying the optimal subset of parameters to prune presents a combinatorial challenge within LLMs' immense parameter space, requiring solutions that efficiently balance competing objectives across the frontiers of model fairness and utility. To address the computational challenges, we explore a search-based program repair approach via randomized simulated annealing. Given the prohibitive evaluation costs in billion-parameter LLMs, we develop surrogate deep neural networks that efficiently model the relationship between attention head states (active/inactive) and their corresponding fairness/utility metrics. This allows us to perform optimization over the surrogate models and efficiently identify optimal subsets of attention heads for selective pruning rather than directly searching through the LLM parameter space. This paper introduces Attention Pruning, a fairness-aware surrogate simulated annealing approach to prune attention heads in LLMs that disproportionately contribute to bias while minimally impacting overall model utility. Our experiments show that Attention Pruning achieves up to $40\%$ reduction in gender bias and outperforms the state-of-the-art bias mitigation strategies.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ATTENTION PRUNING (AP), a novel post-processing technique for mitigating biases in Large Language Models (LLMs) through selective attention head pruning. The approach leverages surrogate Deep Neural Networks (DNNs) to approximate the fairness/utility trade-off associated with different attention head configurations, allowing for efficient exploration of the vast combinatorial search space using Simulated Annealing (SA).  AP aims to identify and prune attention heads that disproportionately contribute to biases while minimizing the impact on overall model utility. The authors demonstrate through experiments that AP reduces gender bias, often outperforming state-of-the-art methods, and generalizes to other social biases.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the combination of several techniques applied to a relevant and important problem: bias mitigation in LLMs. The core idea of using surrogate DNNs to drastically reduce the computational cost of evaluating different pruning configurations is a significant step forward. Specifically, several aspects of the work contribute to its novelty. First, the specific adaptation of simulated annealing for attention head pruning, guided by surrogate models, is novel. Additionally, the methodology of training the surrogate models to reflect the behavior of LLMs with different attention head configurations is a specific contribution.
*   **Significance:** Bias in LLMs is a major concern, particularly as these models are deployed in sensitive real-world applications.  The paper tackles this problem head-on, offering a practical post-processing solution that doesn't require retraining the entire model. The demonstrated reduction in gender bias and the generalization to other social biases highlights the potential impact of AP. The effectiveness of surrogate DNN models for modeling LLM behavior and the computational speed-up it offers is particularly valuable for researchers facing resource constraints. The approach is designed to improve the trustworthiness and responsible use of LLMs, which is very important.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Efficient Search:** The use of surrogate DNNs to model the fairness/utility trade-off is a computationally efficient approach to attention head pruning, enabling exploration of the vast combinatorial space.
    *   **Empirical Validation:**  The paper includes comprehensive experiments on multiple LLMs, demonstrating the effectiveness of AP in reducing bias and outperforming existing methods.
    *   **Generalization:** The results show that reducing gender bias using AP also positively impacts other social biases.
    *   **Design Considerations:** The paper provides a clear understanding of the design considerations related to fairness and utility tradeoffs by showing results from experiments using various configurations of AP's hyperparameters.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Bias Metric:** The evaluation primarily focuses on the HolisticBias metric for gender bias. While this is consistent with previous work, exploring other bias metrics (especially task-specific extrinsic bias) would strengthen the conclusions.
    *   **Dependence on Surrogate Models:** The performance of AP heavily relies on the accuracy of the surrogate DNNs.  While the paper demonstrates low MSE on the validation set, a deeper analysis of the surrogate models' limitations and potential biases would be beneficial. Furthermore, better clarity on the data collection process for training the surrogate models is required. Specifically, the amount of data is too high and the paper lacks appropriate information on how to collect the data samples.
    *   **Hyperparameter Tuning:** The number of hyperparameters to tune (epsilon, bounds on the number of pruned heads, etc.) introduces complexity, and the paper only partially explores the impact of different hyperparameter configurations.
    *   **Limited Scalability Discussion:** It's unclear how the surrogate DNN training scales to even larger LLMs (e.g., models with hundreds of billions of parameters). The paper mentions an NVIDIA RTX A6000 GPU for surrogate DNN inference. It does not discuss how this resource can be achieved practically.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in bias mitigation for LLMs. The AP approach offers a feasible and effective way to improve fairness in pre-trained models without requiring expensive retraining. The idea of using surrogate models can be extended to other post-processing techniques and potentially to other responsible AI requirements, like improving LLM robustness.  Furthermore, the paper could spur research into better techniques for training surrogate models that accurately capture the complex behavior of LLMs. The authors may also consider studying the biases that are most common in LLMs and how different approaches can be used to mitigate them.
    * **Reproducibility:** The limited details related to training the surrogate models pose a big concern for the reproducibility of the work. Additionally, the authors need to provide access to data samples to ensure proper verification of the algorithm.

**Justification for Score:**

Overall, the paper makes a significant contribution to the field of bias mitigation in LLMs. The combination of surrogate models and simulated annealing offers a novel and efficient approach. The empirical results are compelling, demonstrating that AP can reduce bias and often outperform existing techniques. However, the weaknesses related to dependence on the chosen bias metric, hyperparameter tuning, and reliance on the accuracy of the surrogate DNNs lead to a slightly lower score. I'm giving it a score of 7 to reflect its novel approach and practical impact, and to encourage the authors to better justify their claims through future experiments.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### A Vision Centric Remote Sensing Benchmark
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15816v1)
- **Authors**: Abduljaleel Adejumo, Faegheh Yeganli, Clifford Broni-bediako, Aoran Xiao, Naoto Yokoya, Mennatullah Siam
- **Abstract**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have achieved remarkable success in vision-language tasks but their remote sensing (RS) counterpart are relatively under explored. Unlike natural images, RS imagery presents unique challenges that current MLLMs struggle to handle, particularly in visual grounding and spatial reasoning. This study investigates the limitations of CLIP-based MLLMs in RS, highlighting their failure to differentiate visually distinct yet semantically similar RS images. To address this, we introduce a remote sensing multimodal visual patterns (RSMMVP) benchmark. It is designed to evaluate MLLMs in RS tasks by identifying the CLIP-blind pairs, where CLIP-based models incorrectly assign high similarity scores to visually distinct RS images. Through a visual question answering (VQA) evaluation, we analyze the performance of state-of-the-art MLLMs, revealing significant limitations in RS specific representation learning. The results provide valuable insights into the weaknesses of CLIP-based visual encoding and offer a foundation for future research to develop more effective MLLMs tailored for remote sensing applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RSMMVP, a new benchmark designed to evaluate the capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) in the domain of remote sensing (RS). The core of the benchmark is based on the concept of "CLIP-blind pairs," where CLIP-based models incorrectly assign high similarity scores to visually distinct RS images. RSMMVP includes a Visual Question Answering (VQA) task built around these CLIP-blind pairs to assess MLLMs' ability to perform visual grounding and spatial reasoning in RS imagery. The paper presents experimental results showing that state-of-the-art MLLMs struggle with the RSMMVP benchmark, highlighting the limitations of CLIP-based visual encoders in capturing fine-grained details in RS images. The authors conclude that there is a need for RS-specific MLLMs that can better handle the complexities of RS data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The concept of using "CLIP-blind pairs" to assess MLLMs is not entirely new (the paper itself acknowledges inspiration from prior work). However, its application to the remote sensing domain and the creation of a benchmark *specifically* designed around this concept adds novelty. The specific benchmark construction and the VQA tasks targeting RS-specific challenges are also novel contributions. However, the core idea of evaluating limitations of CLIP-based encoders is somewhat incremental.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in several aspects:

    *   **Highlighting limitations:** It clearly demonstrates the limitations of general-purpose MLLMs in the specialized domain of RS, a crucial area with increasing importance.

    *   **Domain-specific Focus:** It underscores the need for domain adaptation of MLLMs, specifically tailored for RS data, rather than relying solely on models pre-trained on natural images.

    *   **Benchmark Creation:** It provides a valuable resource (RSMMVP) for the community to evaluate and compare different MLLM architectures and training strategies for RS applications. The publicly available dataset will encourage further research.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition: The paper clearly identifies a crucial problem in applying general MLLMs to RS.

    *   Well-designed benchmark: The RSMMVP benchmark, based on CLIP-blind pairs, is a well-thought-out approach to assess specific limitations.

    *   Rigorous evaluation: The evaluation is thorough, comparing multiple state-of-the-art MLLMs.

    *   Detailed error analysis: The categorization of errors based on visual patterns provides valuable insights into the weaknesses of the models.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Limited scope: The study primarily focuses on CLIP-based models.  While CLIP is widely used, exploring alternatives, or proposing improvements to CLIP tailored for RS, would have strengthened the contribution.

    *   Limited MLLM comparison: Although the paper compares multiple models, some of them are LLaVA variants. Wider exploration with different MLLM architectures could provide a richer understanding.

    *   Incremental nature: As stated above, while the application to RS is novel, the core idea behind the method is not entirely original.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field by identifying and quantifying the limitations of current MLLMs in the remote sensing domain. The RSMMVP benchmark is a useful tool for future research in this area. While the core concept of CLIP-blind pairs isn't entirely novel, its specific application and benchmark design for RS are significant. The research has the potential to influence the development of more effective MLLMs tailored for geospatial applications.

### EDEN: Enhanced Diffusion for High-quality Large-motion Video Frame Interpolation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15831v1)
- **Authors**: Zihao Zhang, Haoran Chen, Haoyu Zhao, Guansong Lu, Yanwei Fu, Hang Xu, Zuxuan Wu
- **Abstract**: Handling complex or nonlinear motion patterns has long posed challenges for video frame interpolation. Although recent advances in diffusion-based methods offer improvements over traditional optical flow-based approaches, they still struggle to generate sharp, temporally consistent frames in scenarios with large motion. To address this limitation, we introduce EDEN, an Enhanced Diffusion for high-quality large-motion vidEo frame iNterpolation. Our approach first utilizes a transformer-based tokenizer to produce refined latent representations of the intermediate frames for diffusion models. We then enhance the diffusion transformer with temporal attention across the process and incorporate a start-end frame difference embedding to guide the generation of dynamic motion. Extensive experiments demonstrate that EDEN achieves state-of-the-art results across popular benchmarks, including nearly a 10% LPIPS reduction on DAVIS and SNU-FILM, and an 8% improvement on DAIN-HD.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EDEN, an Enhanced Diffusion model for high-quality large-motion video frame interpolation (VFI).  EDEN addresses the limitations of existing diffusion-based VFI methods, which often struggle with generating sharp, temporally consistent frames when dealing with significant motion.  The approach incorporates a transformer-based tokenizer to create refined latent representations, enhances the diffusion transformer with temporal attention, and uses a start-end frame difference embedding to guide motion generation. The authors demonstrate state-of-the-art results on popular benchmarks like DAVIS, SNU-FILM, and DAIN-HD.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper presents several novel components, but their individual novelty varies.

*   **Transformer-based Tokenizer:** The use of a transformer-based tokenizer specifically for video *frame interpolation* is a key contribution. While image tokenization exists, tailoring it and showing its superiority over standard 2D convolutional VAEs for this *particular* task is relatively novel. It is built on the recent insight from [64] demonstrating better image information representation using 1D tokenization rather than 2D grid representations.
*   **Temporal Attention in Diffusion Transformer:** Integrating temporal attention *throughout* the diffusion transformer, similar to the tokenizer architecture, represents a more holistic context integration. This is not entirely new but is an effective architectural choice specific to the VFI context. Adding a start-end difference context is a similar approach.
*   **Multi-resolution Fine-tuning:** Multi-resolution training exists but its adaptation and effectiveness in this specific diffusion-based VFI setting is a worthwhile contribution, given the diverse resolutions found in real-world videos.
*   **End-to-End Framework:**  The integration of these components (tokenizer, enhanced diffusion, and fine-tuning) into a complete framework, EDEN, represents a cohesive approach.

**Significance:**

*   **State-of-the-Art Results:** The paper presents compelling quantitative results, achieving significant improvements over previous state-of-the-art methods, especially on challenging large-motion datasets like DAVIS and DAIN-HD.  This demonstrates the effectiveness of the proposed techniques in addressing the core issue of large motion handling. The relative performance increases are impressive, showing near 10% reduction of LPIPS for DAVIS and SNU-FILM and an 8% improvement on DAIN-HD.
*   **Architectural Insights:** The ablation studies provide insights into the importance of various components (e.g., tokenizer, temporal attention, start-end difference).  These insights can inform future research in this area.
*   **Practical Impact:** Video frame interpolation has numerous practical applications (slow motion, animation, video compression).  Improving the quality and robustness of VFI, particularly in scenarios with large motion, has broad potential impact.
*   **Relatively Fast Inference:** The paper states their method to be significantly faster compared to earlier diffusion approaches. Also, by using just 2 diffusion steps, EDEN provides a significant decrease in the time needed in reference to prior models based on diffusion. This is relevant as it shows potential for the method to become widely used in practical situations.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Blurring in Fine Details:** The limitations regarding blurring when handling fast changes in fine details (e.g., text) are acknowledged, but more detailed investigation and potential solutions would strengthen the paper. While they have discussed that the implementation with pixel shuffle to create intermediate frames may be the source of the artifacts, they only mention the addition of the feature as a possible solution, requiring to fully test said fix.
*   **Limited Novelty of Individual Components:**  While the integration is novel, some individual components (e.g., diffusion transformers, attention mechanisms) are based on existing work. This limits the paper's overall theoretical contribution.
*   **Computational Cost:** While the paper states that they have significantly reduced the computational time from the previous diffusion methods, they still claim that EDEN is slower than methods relying on optical flow.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a strong engineering contribution with significant practical implications. The architectural innovations (transformer-based tokenizer, specific adaptations to diffusion transformers) lead to substantial improvements in performance on a relevant and challenging problem. The detailed ablation studies provide valuable insights.  While the individual components might not be ground-breaking in isolation, their integration within the EDEN framework and their effective application to VFI constitute a valuable advancement. Given the impact the paper makes, it deserves a strong score.

Score: 7.5

### Fùxì: A Benchmark for Evaluating Language Models on Ancient Chinese Text Understanding and Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15837v1)
- **Authors**: Shangqing Zhao, Yuhao Zhou, Yupei Ren, Zhe Chen, Chenghao Jia, Fang Zhe, Zhaogaung Long, Shu Liu, Man Lan
- **Abstract**: Ancient Chinese text processing presents unique challenges for large language models (LLMs) due to its distinct linguistic features, complex structural constraints, and rich cultural context. While existing benchmarks have primarily focused on evaluating comprehension through multiple-choice questions, there remains a critical gap in assessing models' generative capabilities in classical Chinese. We introduce F\`ux\`i, a comprehensive benchmark that evaluates both understanding and generation capabilities across 21 diverse tasks. Our benchmark distinguishes itself through three key contributions: (1) balanced coverage of both comprehension and generation tasks, including novel tasks like poetry composition and couplet completion, (2) specialized evaluation metrics designed specifically for classical Chinese text generation, combining rule-based verification with fine-tuned LLM evaluators, and (3) a systematic assessment framework that considers both linguistic accuracy and cultural authenticity. Through extensive evaluation of state-of-the-art LLMs, we reveal significant performance gaps between understanding and generation tasks, with models achieving promising results in comprehension but struggling considerably in generation tasks, particularly those requiring deep cultural knowledge and adherence to classical formats. Our findings highlight the current limitations in ancient Chinese text processing and provide insights for future model development. The benchmark, evaluation toolkit, and baseline results are publicly available to facilitate research in this domain.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Fùxì: A Benchmark for Evaluating Language Models on Ancient Chinese Text Understanding and Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Fùxì, a novel benchmark designed to evaluate the capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) in understanding and generating classical Chinese text. This is an important area because current LLMs, while performing well in modern Chinese, struggle with the unique linguistic features, structural constraints, and cultural context of ancient Chinese.  Fùxì comprises 21 diverse tasks covering both comprehension and generation, and includes innovative tasks like poetry composition and couplet completion. The benchmark is also distinguished by its specialized evaluation metrics tailored for classical Chinese text generation, combining rule-based verification and fine-tuned LLM evaluators. The authors conduct extensive experiments with state-of-the-art LLMs, highlighting the performance gaps between understanding and generation tasks, and providing insights for future research.  The benchmark, evaluation toolkit, and baseline results are made publicly available.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper offers a significant and needed contribution to the field of NLP. Existing benchmarks primarily focus on modern Chinese and understanding-based tasks (mainly multiple-choice). Fùxì's novelty lies in:

*   **Focus on Ancient Chinese:** Addressing a specific and challenging subfield of NLP.
*   **Balanced Comprehension and Generation:**  Prioritizing generation tasks which are currently under-evaluated in the context of Ancient Chinese.
*   **Innovative Task Design:** Introducing tasks explicitly tailored to classical Chinese, like poetry generation and couplet completion, which assess nuanced understanding of literary conventions.
*   **Specialized Evaluation Metrics:** Tackling the difficult problem of evaluating ancient Chinese text generation by a hybrid method of rule-based systems and fine-tuned LLM evaluators.
*   **Publicly Available Resource:** A vital step in fostering research and further development in this domain.

**Significance:** The significance of this work stems from:

*   **Cultural Heritage Preservation:** Ancient Chinese texts are a vital part of cultural heritage, and better NLP tools can make them more accessible and understood.
*   **Pushing the Boundaries of LLMs:** Evaluating LLMs on a complex and linguistically distinct language like ancient Chinese exposes their limitations and encourages improvements in areas like cultural knowledge integration and format adherence.
*   **Setting a Standard for Evaluation:**  Fùxì provides a structured framework and evaluation protocols that can be adopted and extended by other researchers in the field.
*   **Insights into Model Strengths and Weaknesses:** The experiments provide valuable insights into the capabilities and shortcomings of various LLM architectures in handling this specific linguistic domain. The revealed gap between comprehension and generation performance is particularly noteworthy.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Task Coverage:** The inclusion of 21 diverse tasks makes Fùxì a well-rounded benchmark.
*   **Careful Dataset Curation:**  The authors mention manual curation and automated processes for data construction, ensuring data quality and reliability.
*   **Thorough Experiments:** The experiments include a variety of LLMs, including open-source and closed-source models, and both zero-shot and few-shot settings.
*   **Robust Evaluation:** The development and validation of an LLM-based evaluator for open-ended generation tasks is a key strength. The correlation with human judgments adds credibility to the automatic evaluation.
*   **Clear Articulation of Limitations:**  The authors acknowledge the limitations of their evaluation metrics and task coverage, which encourages further research in these areas.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Evaluation Metric Bias:** While the LLM-based evaluator is validated, there remains the potential for subtle biases, particularly in subjective tasks where cultural authenticity is crucial. Further refinement and diversification of the training data for the evaluator could improve this.
*   **Limited Aesthetical Evaluation:**  The focus on format correctness in poetry and couplet generation, while a reasonable starting point, neglects the aesthetic qualities of these literary forms. Future work could explore ways to integrate aesthetic evaluation.
*   **Potential Data Biases:**  While data curation is mentioned, it's important to consider the potential for biases present in the original data sources used to create the benchmark, which could influence model performance.

**Potential Influence:**

Fùxì has the potential to become a standard benchmark for evaluating LLMs on ancient Chinese text processing. It can drive research in areas like:

*   Developing more culturally aware LLMs.
*   Improving the generation capabilities of LLMs, particularly for creative tasks.
*   Creating more effective evaluation metrics for nuanced linguistic tasks.
*   Exploring cross-lingual transfer learning techniques for ancient languages.

**Justification of Score:**

I assign a score of **8.5**.  The paper makes a significant contribution by providing a novel and comprehensive benchmark for a challenging and important subfield of NLP.  The tasks are thoughtfully designed, and the evaluation methodology is robust.  The public availability of the benchmark is a major strength that will foster further research. The weaknesses related to evaluation metric biases and limitations in aesthetic evaluation are acknowledged and provide clear directions for future work. While not a perfect benchmark, Fùxì fills a critical gap and will undoubtedly have a positive impact on the field.

**Score: 8.5**
