### Enhancing LLM Code Generation with Ensembles: A Similarity-Based Selection Approach
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15838v1)
- **Authors**: Tarek Mahmud, Bin Duan, Corina Pasareanu, Guowei Yang
- **Abstract**: Ensemble learning has been widely used in machine learning to improve model robustness, accuracy, and generalization, but has not yet been applied to code generation tasks with large language models (LLMs). We propose an ensemble approach for LLMs in code generation. Instead of relying on the output of a single model, we generate multiple candidate programs from different LLMs and apply a structured voting mechanism to select the most reliable solution. For voting, we compute syntactic and semantic similarity using CodeBLEU and behavioral equivalence using CrossHair's differential behavior analysis. By aggregating these similarity scores, we select the program that best aligns with the consensus among the candidates. We show through experiments that our ensemble approach consistently outperforms standalone LLMs on the well-known HumanEval and the more challenging LiveCodeBench datasets, achieving an accuracy of 90.2% and 50.2%, respectively, on the two datasets. In comparison, the best-performing LLM (GPT-4o) has an accuracy of 83.5% and 43.4%, respectively. Furthermore, even when restricted to free open-source models, our method achieves an accuracy of 80.5% and 41.6%, respectively, demonstrating the viability of our approach in resource-constrained settings.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EnsLLM, an ensemble-based approach designed to enhance the reliability and accuracy of code generation using Large Language Models (LLMs).  Instead of relying on a single LLM's output, EnsLLM generates multiple candidate programs using diverse LLMs and then employs a structured voting mechanism to select the most reliable solution. This voting process is driven by a similarity-based ranking that considers syntactic and semantic similarity (using CodeBLEU) and behavioral equivalence (using CrossHair for differential analysis). The paper presents experimental results on the HumanEval and LiveCodeBench datasets, demonstrating that EnsLLM consistently outperforms standalone LLMs, even when restricted to using only free, open-source models. The authors analyze failure cases and show how correct programs reinforce each other in the ensemble.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The core idea of using an ensemble of LLMs for code generation isn't entirely groundbreaking, as ensemble methods are widely used in machine learning generally. However, the specific approach EnsLLM takes *is* novel in its application to code generation and its structured voting mechanism.  The paper's real contribution lies in the *specific* design of the voting mechanism:

*   **Integration of CodeBLEU and CrossHair:** The combination of a static code similarity metric (CodeBLEU) with dynamic behavioral analysis (CrossHair) is a solid and well-justified design choice. It addresses the limitations of relying solely on static code analysis or solely on limited test case coverage. This integrated approach enhances the robustness of code selection.

*   **Pairwise Similarity for Ranking:** The use of pairwise similarity between generated programs to derive an overall ranking is a useful way to leverage the strengths of multiple potential solutions. This approach indirectly infers confidence in generated code by assessing its alignment with a consensus of other generated outputs.

**Significance:**

The paper's significance lies in its demonstrated ability to improve the accuracy and reliability of code generation, which is a critical aspect of leveraging LLMs in software development.  The findings that EnsLLM outperforms standalone LLMs, including GPT-40, on both HumanEval and LiveCodeBench, provide compelling evidence of its effectiveness.  Furthermore, the ability of EnsLLM to maintain reasonable accuracy even when using only free, open-source models is significant from a practical and accessibility perspective.

**Strengths:**

*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper presents thorough experimental results on well-established benchmarks, providing clear evidence of EnsLLM's effectiveness.
*   **Well-Defined Approach:**  The ensemble method and voting mechanism are clearly described, making the approach replicable.
*   **Addressing Limitations:** The paper acknowledges the limitations of the approach (e.g., dependence on initial LLM quality, failure when all LLMs generate incorrect code), demonstrating intellectual honesty.
*   **Ablation Study:** The ablation study (RQ2) effectively isolates the contributions of CodeBLEU and CrossHair, providing valuable insights into the importance of each component.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:** While the specific combination and application are novel, the *fundamental* idea of using an ensemble is not.
*   **Computational Cost:**  The paper doesn't deeply address the computational cost of generating multiple programs and performing pairwise similarity analysis. This could be a limiting factor in practice. The use of a GPU is mentioned, but no specific timings are given. This makes it difficult to assess real-world practicality.
*   **Limited Scope of Metrics:** While CodeBLEU and CrossHair are valuable, exploring other code quality metrics (e.g., complexity, maintainability, security vulnerabilities) could further enhance the ensemble's effectiveness.
*   **Tie Breaking in Voting:**  The current tie-breaking mechanism relies on the number of counterexamples found by CrossHair, and then uses random selection. If there are two implementations that are syntactically correct, semantically correct, and *functionally equivalent*, but *one* of those implementations is far more performant or more concise, then the random selection is less than optimal. This may also be true of using number of counterexamples as a tie breaker.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence the field of LLM-based code generation by demonstrating the benefits of ensembling and providing a concrete approach for combining different code quality metrics. It could also inspire further research on more sophisticated voting mechanisms and the integration of other relevant metrics. The finding that EnsLLM works well with free LLMs also has implications for accessibility and democratizing the use of these tools.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of 7. The paper's significant contribution lies in the practical demonstration of its approach, demonstrating a clear improvement over standalone LLMs. The specific combination of CodeBLEU and CrossHair is a well-reasoned design decision that demonstrates a useful innovation on previous work. However, the novelty is somewhat incremental due to the existing body of work in ensemble learning. While computationally expensive, the use of a similarity based ranking may result in greater efficiency, as models effectively 'agree' with each other. There are also improvements to be made in the tie-breaking logic and exploration of additional code quality metrics. These would likely improve the accuracy of the ensemble, and enhance its practical effectiveness.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### Automatic Generation of Safety-compliant Linear Temporal Logic via Large Language Model: A Self-supervised Framework
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15840v1)
- **Authors**: Junle Li, Meiqi Tian, Bingzhuo Zhong
- **Abstract**: Ensuring safety in cyber-physical systems (CPS) poses a significant challenge, especially when converting high-level tasks described by natural language into formal specifications like Linear Temporal Logic (LTL). In particular, the compliance of formal languages with respect to safety restrictions imposed on CPS is crucial for system safety. In this paper, we introduce AutoSafeLTL, a self-supervised framework that utilizes large language models (LLMs) to automate the generation of safety-compliant LTL. Our approach integrates a Language Inclusion check with an automated counterexample-guided feedback and modification mechanism, establishing a pipeline that verifies the safety-compliance of the resulting LTL while preserving its logical consistency and semantic accuracy. To enhance the framework's understanding and correction capabilities, we incorporate two additional Agent LLMs. Experimental results demonstrate that AutoSafeLTL effectively guarantees safety-compliance for generated LTL, achieving a 0% violation rate against imposed safety constraints.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AutoSafeLTL, a self-supervised framework that leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) to automatically generate safety-compliant Linear Temporal Logic (LTL) specifications from natural language descriptions of desired tasks in cyber-physical systems (CPS). The key innovation is the integration of a Language Inclusion check with an automated counterexample-guided feedback and modification mechanism. This pipeline verifies the safety compliance of generated LTL formulas against predefined safety restrictions (Base Rules) while maintaining logical consistency and semantic accuracy. The framework incorporates two additional Agent LLMs to improve understanding and correction capabilities. Experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of AutoSafeLTL in guaranteeing safety compliance, achieving a 0% violation rate against imposed safety constraints.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**  The paper addresses a crucial but often overlooked aspect in automatic LTL generation: ensuring *safety compliance* with predefined system constraints. While existing works focus on semantic consistency between natural language and LTL, this paper explicitly targets the conflict between generated LTL and safety rules. This is a significant advancement because non-compliant LTL can lead to dangerous behavior in safety-critical systems. The integration of formal verification techniques (Language Inclusion Check) with LLMs in a self-supervised loop is also a novel approach.

**Significance:** The practical implications of this work are substantial. Automating the generation of safety-compliant LTL specifications can significantly reduce the effort and expertise required for formally verifying and synthesizing controllers for CPS. This can lead to more reliable and safer autonomous systems, industrial automation, and medical devices.  The framework's ability to provide *formal guarantees* of safety compliance, a feature absent in many existing LLM-based approaches, is particularly valuable.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clearly defined problem:** The paper identifies a gap in existing LTL generation techniques.
*   **Novel approach:** The combination of LLMs with formal verification in a self-supervised loop is innovative.
*   **Practical relevance:** The work has direct applications in safety-critical domains.
*   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of AutoSafeLTL, particularly the 0% violation rate.
*   **Ablation study:** The ablation experiments convincingly demonstrate the importance of each component of the proposed framework, particularly the Agent LLMs.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Data and Scenario:** The experiments primarily focus on a traffic scenario, which is not a limitation in itself, but this could give rise to a limited scope in evaluating real-world implementations.  The experiments could benefit from a broader range of CPS applications to showcase the framework's generalizability.
*   **Reliance on existing tools:** The framework relies on external tools like Spot and RABIT. While these tools are well-established, the stability and performance of AutoSafeLTL are contingent on these tools. The paper acknowledges this limitation.
*   **Computational overhead:** Iterative modification loop might be computationally expensive for certain CPS systems depending on complexity and scale.
*   **Over reliance on LLMs**: While the inclusion of Agent LLMs improve the performance, there is still a question on how LLMs can be made to be more robust in interpreting formal aspects of LTL specifications.

**Impact:**

The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field of formal methods for CPS. By providing a way to automatically generate safety-compliant specifications, it can lower the barrier to entry for formal verification and synthesis. This can lead to wider adoption of these techniques in industry, ultimately resulting in safer and more reliable systems. Future works may build on this framework by incorporating more sophisticated verification techniques or by extending it to handle more complex CPS scenarios. The dataset that is being created might also have an impact in furthering research.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to a critical problem in CPS. The integration of LLMs with formal verification techniques is a significant contribution. While the experiments are somewhat limited, the results are compelling, and the potential impact of the work is high. The paper demonstrates a clear understanding of both LLMs and formal verification.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.LO
- **Score**: 8/10

### Uncertainty Quantification and Confidence Calibration in Large Language Models: A Survey
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15850v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaoou Liu, Tiejin Chen, Longchao Da, Chacha Chen, Zhen Lin, Hua Wei
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) excel in text generation, reasoning, and decision-making, enabling their adoption in high-stakes domains such as healthcare, law, and transportation. However, their reliability is a major concern, as they often produce plausible but incorrect responses. Uncertainty quantification (UQ) enhances trustworthiness by estimating confidence in outputs, enabling risk mitigation and selective prediction. However, traditional UQ methods struggle with LLMs due to computational constraints and decoding inconsistencies. Moreover, LLMs introduce unique uncertainty sources, such as input ambiguity, reasoning path divergence, and decoding stochasticity, that extend beyond classical aleatoric and epistemic uncertainty. To address this, we introduce a new taxonomy that categorizes UQ methods based on computational efficiency and uncertainty dimensions (input, reasoning, parameter, and prediction uncertainty). We evaluate existing techniques, assess their real-world applicability, and identify open challenges, emphasizing the need for scalable, interpretable, and robust UQ approaches to enhance LLM reliability.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the provided OCR of the paper "Uncertainty Quantification and Confidence Calibration in Large Language Models: A Survey". Here's a summary and critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a survey of uncertainty quantification (UQ) and confidence calibration techniques for large language models (LLMs). It highlights the increasing importance of reliability in LLMs, especially in high-stakes domains.  The authors propose a new taxonomy to categorize UQ methods based on computational efficiency and uncertainty dimensions: input, reasoning, parameter, and prediction uncertainty. The survey evaluates existing UQ techniques in LLMs, assesses their real-world applicability, and identifies open challenges, emphasizing the need for scalable, interpretable, and robust UQ approaches. The authors differentiate between uncertainty and confidence and discuss evaluation methods tailored to the nuances of natural language generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its proposed taxonomy that focuses on uncertainty dimensions *specific to LLMs*. This is a departure from traditional UQ surveys that often apply classical aleatoric/epistemic classifications, which are insufficient for capturing the complexities introduced by LLMs (e.g., input ambiguity, reasoning path divergence, and decoding stochasticity).  The paper's focus on dimensions like reasoning uncertainty fills a gap in existing literature. Moreover, it emphasizes connections between areas (such as safety and robotics) with UQ, which is a novel angle.

*   **Significance:**  The significance stems from the increasing deployment of LLMs in critical applications.  The authors correctly point out that the unreliability of LLMs, particularly the tendency to generate plausible but incorrect responses, is a major obstacle to widespread adoption. By providing a structured overview of UQ techniques and highlighting the limitations of existing methods, the survey contributes to a more reliable and trustworthy application of LLMs. The identification of under-explored areas, like reasoning uncertainty, is particularly valuable.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Taxonomy:** The proposed taxonomy provides a useful framework for understanding and categorizing UQ methods in the context of LLMs.
    *   **Comprehensive Coverage:** The survey covers a reasonably wide range of existing techniques, including both black-box and white-box methods.
    *   **Focus on LLM-Specific Challenges:**  The paper directly addresses the unique challenges posed by LLMs, differentiating them from traditional machine learning models.
    *   **Practical Relevance:** The discussion of applications and open challenges enhances the paper's practical relevance and motivates future research.
    *   **Emphasis on Evaluation:** Explicit discussion of UQ evaluation methods and the issues encountered in NLG tasks adds significant value.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Depth of Evaluation:** While the survey covers a wide range of techniques, the depth of evaluation for each method could be improved. A more critical comparison of the strengths and weaknesses of different methods within each category would be beneficial. Some areas appear to have fewer methods discussed compared to others, leading to an imbalance in the survey.
    *   **Limited Empirical Validation:** The survey primarily focuses on reviewing existing literature and theoretical aspects. The authors do not present new experimental results or compare existing methods on common benchmarks.
    *   **Uneven focus across Dimensions**: The paper emphasizes the need to consider distinct types of uncertainty, however, it appears to be more thorough in examining prediction uncertainty while relatively less on input and parameter uncertainties.
    *   **Subjectivity in Taxonomy:** There might be some degree of subjectivity in assigning methods to specific categories in the taxonomy. The authors could provide more justification for these assignments.

*   **Potential Influence:** This survey has the potential to significantly influence the field by:

    *   Providing a valuable resource for researchers and practitioners working on UQ for LLMs.
    *   Motivating further research into under-explored areas like reasoning uncertainty.
    *   Guiding the development of new UQ techniques tailored to the specific characteristics of LLMs.
    *   Promoting the adoption of UQ in high-stakes applications of LLMs.

**Justification of Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7.5**. While the paper is very valuable, the lack of empirical validation and the limitations in the depth of evaluation prevent it from scoring higher. The proposed taxonomy and focus on LLM-specific challenges are strong points, indicating significant novelty. The paper addresses a very relevant problem and has the potential to drive future research. A score of 8 would indicate an exceptional, transformative contribution that is likely to shift the trajectory of the field. Because of weaknesses in certain areas, the 7.5 is more appropriate.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Zero-1-to-A: Zero-Shot One Image to Animatable Head Avatars Using Video Diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15851v1)
- **Authors**: Zhou Zhenglin, Ma Fan, Fan Hehe, Chua Tat-Seng
- **Abstract**: Animatable head avatar generation typically requires extensive data for training. To reduce the data requirements, a natural solution is to leverage existing data-free static avatar generation methods, such as pre-trained diffusion models with score distillation sampling (SDS), which align avatars with pseudo ground-truth outputs from the diffusion model. However, directly distilling 4D avatars from video diffusion often leads to over-smooth results due to spatial and temporal inconsistencies in the generated video. To address this issue, we propose Zero-1-to-A, a robust method that synthesizes a spatial and temporal consistency dataset for 4D avatar reconstruction using the video diffusion model. Specifically, Zero-1-to-A iteratively constructs video datasets and optimizes animatable avatars in a progressive manner, ensuring that avatar quality increases smoothly and consistently throughout the learning process. This progressive learning involves two stages: (1) Spatial Consistency Learning fixes expressions and learns from front-to-side views, and (2) Temporal Consistency Learning fixes views and learns from relaxed to exaggerated expressions, generating 4D avatars in a simple-to-complex manner. Extensive experiments demonstrate that Zero-1-to-A improves fidelity, animation quality, and rendering speed compared to existing diffusion-based methods, providing a solution for lifelike avatar creation. Code is publicly available at: https://github.com/ZhenglinZhou/Zero-1-to-A.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Zero-1-to-A, a novel method for generating animatable 3D head avatars from a single image, leveraging pre-trained video diffusion models. The core idea is to address the spatial and temporal inconsistencies that arise when directly distilling 4D avatars from video diffusion models using score distillation sampling (SDS). Zero-1-to-A proposes a two-stage progressive learning approach combined with a "Symbiotic Generation" (SymGEN) framework. SymGEN iteratively constructs spatially and temporally consistent datasets by integrating head avatars into video generation, refining both the dataset and the avatar in a mutually beneficial cycle. The two-stage progressive learning decouples the task into Spatial Consistency Learning (fixing expressions and learning front-to-side views) and Temporal Consistency Learning (fixing camera and learning relaxed-to-exaggerated expressions).  Experiments demonstrate improved fidelity, animation quality, and rendering speed compared to existing diffusion-based methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper exhibits reasonable novelty in its approach to 4D avatar generation. The core innovation lies in the SymGEN framework and the two-stage progressive learning strategy. While SDS-based methods are not entirely new in this domain, the specific application to 4D avatar generation from a *single* image, along with the mechanisms for addressing inconsistencies in video diffusion, is a distinct contribution.  The progressive learning strategy, although conceptually intuitive, is well-motivated by the observed limitations of directly distilling from video diffusion and helps overcome the chicken-and-egg problem in dataset and avatar refinement.

*   **Significance:** The paper tackles a significant problem in avatar generation: reducing the need for large, expensive training datasets. By leveraging pre-trained video diffusion models, Zero-1-to-A provides a data-efficient alternative. The improved fidelity, animation quality, and rendering speed demonstrated in the experiments make it a potentially practical solution. It contributes to the field by exploring image-conditioned 4D avatar generation, a relatively underexplored area compared to text-conditioned approaches. However, its reliance on and adaptation of established methods limits its groundbreaking potential.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-motivated approach:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of direct SDS distillation and provides a logical rationale for the proposed solutions.
    *   **Comprehensive experiments:**  The experiments are thorough, including qualitative comparisons with state-of-the-art methods, quantitative evaluations (CLIP score, ID consistency, smoothness, rendering speed), and ablation studies to validate design choices. The additional experiments in the supplementary material offer a deeper dive.
    *   **Practical benefits:** The demonstrated improvements in fidelity, animation, and rendering speed suggest that the method is practically useful.
    *   **Clear writing:** The paper is generally well-written and easy to follow.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on established methods:**  The method relies heavily on pre-trained video diffusion models and SDS, limiting its fundamental innovation. While it refines and adapts these techniques, it doesn't introduce truly radical concepts.
    *   **Limited generalizability:** While the "in-the-wild" cases are considered, further exploration into very diverse and challenging single input images is necessary.
    *   **Limited scope for non-humanoid avatars:** The constraints of FLAME meshes in shaping the Gaussian points is a substantial constraint for avatar diversity.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's potential influence lies in popularizing the use of SymGEN for 4D avatar generation. Zero-1-to-A can serve as a foundation for future research aimed at further improving the quality, efficiency, and control of avatar generation systems. Further work will need to be done to diversify these avatars and reduce the reliance of mesh-driven 3D Gaussians.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the novelty (reasonable but not revolutionary), significance (contributes to data-efficient avatar generation), strengths (well-motivated, comprehensive experiments, practical benefits, clear writing), and weaknesses (reliance on existing methods, generalizability, limited to mesh-driven facial expression), a score of 6 is appropriate. It's a solid engineering contribution with practical value, but it doesn't represent a paradigm shift in the field.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### DroidTTP: Mapping Android Applications with TTP for Cyber Threat Intelligence
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15866v1)
- **Authors**: Dincy R Arikkat, Vinod P., Rafidha Rehiman K. A., Serena Nicolazzo, Marco Arazzi, Antonino Nocera, Mauro Conti
- **Abstract**: The widespread adoption of Android devices for sensitive operations like banking and communication has made them prime targets for cyber threats, particularly Advanced Persistent Threats (APT) and sophisticated malware attacks. Traditional malware detection methods rely on binary classification, failing to provide insights into adversarial Tactics, Techniques, and Procedures (TTPs). Understanding malware behavior is crucial for enhancing cybersecurity defenses. To address this gap, we introduce DroidTTP, a framework mapping Android malware behaviors to TTPs based on the MITRE ATT&CK framework. Our curated dataset explicitly links MITRE TTPs to Android applications. We developed an automated solution leveraging the Problem Transformation Approach (PTA) and Large Language Models (LLMs) to map applications to both Tactics and Techniques. Additionally, we employed Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) with prompt engineering and LLM fine-tuning for TTP predictions. Our structured pipeline includes dataset creation, hyperparameter tuning, data augmentation, feature selection, model development, and SHAP-based model interpretability. Among LLMs, Llama achieved the highest performance in Tactic classification with a Jaccard Similarity of 0.9583 and Hamming Loss of 0.0182, and in Technique classification with a Jaccard Similarity of 0.9348 and Hamming Loss of 0.0127. However, the Label Powerset XGBoost model outperformed LLMs, achieving a Jaccard Similarity of 0.9893 for Tactic classification and 0.9753 for Technique classification, with a Hamming Loss of 0.0054 and 0.0050, respectively. While XGBoost showed superior performance, the narrow margin highlights the potential of LLM-based approaches in TTP classification.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary**

The paper "DroidTTP: Mapping Android Applications with TTP for Cyber Threat Intelligence" introduces a novel approach for automatically mapping Android malware behaviors to Tactics, Techniques, and Procedures (TTPs) defined by the MITRE ATT&CK framework. The core contributions include a new dataset linking MITRE TTPs to Android applications, an enhanced feature selection strategy for multi-label classification, and the development of automated systems for Tactic and Technique mapping. The authors further explored the application of Large Language Models (LLMs) in TTP prediction using Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) and fine-tuning strategies.  Experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed solutions, particularly the Label Powerset XGBoost model for classification and the potential of fine-tuned LLMs for TTP classification.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper has a moderate degree of novelty.  While malware classification and the use of LLMs in cybersecurity are established areas, the *specific* application of LLMs to map Android malware behavior to MITRE ATT&CK TTPs, *particularly* with the creation of a custom dataset and the combination of PTA approaches with LLMs, is a valuable contribution. A key strength is in the creation of the dataset, which fills a crucial gap. The feature selection methodology also represents a small but useful improvement over standard SelectKBest. The combined PTA is not so new but using it together with feature selection adds small novelty

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work lies in bridging the gap between basic malware detection and actionable threat intelligence. Traditional malware classification focuses on identifying malicious apps but doesn't reveal the attacker's methods. DroidTTP offers a more nuanced understanding, enabling security analysts to better anticipate and defend against cyber threats. The LLM experiments show promise, although the performance is not decisively superior to traditional methods. A significant contribution is the dataset itself, which will serve as a benchmark for future research.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Dataset:** The creation of a labeled dataset specifically for Android malware TTP mapping is a significant strength. Publicly available datasets in this domain are limited.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides a thorough evaluation, comparing various machine learning models, prompt engineering strategies, RAG configurations, and fine-tuning approaches.
    *   **Feature Selection Enhancement:** The proposed feature selection enhances feature relevance from multiple datasets.
    *   **Addresses Practical Need:** The work directly addresses a practical need in the field of cybersecurity by enabling more informed and proactive defense strategies.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **LLM Performance:** While the use of LLMs is explored, the performance gains over traditional machine learning models (XGBoost) are marginal. A greater performance gap would highlight the practical benefits of the system.
    *   **Limited Techniques:** The Technique dataset was limited by available samples which limited the number of techniques.
    *   **Reliance on Static Analysis:** The approach relies primarily on static analysis of Android apps, which may be circumvented by advanced malware using techniques like code obfuscation or dynamic code loading. This makes it a less robust and general approach.
    *   **Limited LLM Experiments:** The LLM experiments are promising but could be expanded. For example, comparing the models' ability to generalize to new, unseen malware families would strengthen the conclusions.
    *   **Dataset Size:** While a valuable contribution, the dataset is relatively small, and larger datasets may yield more robust results.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in Android malware analysis, threat intelligence, and the application of LLMs in cybersecurity. The dataset will likely be used by other researchers as a benchmark.

**Justification of Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7.0** to this paper.

Rationale: The paper provides a solid and useful contribution to the field of Android malware analysis by providing a dataset linking MITRE TTPs to Android applications. It clearly addresses a current gap in malware detection by giving information about attacker behaviour. The use of fine-tuned LLMs offers potential, although the actual results need more to close the performance gap with traditional methods. The limitations such as reliance on static analysis and the need for a larger dataset are important to be considered. Therefore, the paper shows novelty and promise but is not enough for a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 0/10

### TruthLens: Explainable DeepFake Detection for Face Manipulated and Fully Synthetic Data
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15867v1)
- **Authors**: Rohit Kundu, Athula Balachandran, Amit K. Roy-Chowdhury
- **Abstract**: Detecting DeepFakes has become a crucial research area as the widespread use of AI image generators enables the effortless creation of face-manipulated and fully synthetic content, yet existing methods are often limited to binary classification (real vs. fake) and lack interpretability. To address these challenges, we propose TruthLens, a novel and highly generalizable framework for DeepFake detection that not only determines whether an image is real or fake but also provides detailed textual reasoning for its predictions. Unlike traditional methods, TruthLens effectively handles both face-manipulated DeepFakes and fully AI-generated content while addressing fine-grained queries such as "Does the eyes/nose/mouth look real or fake?" The architecture of TruthLens combines the global contextual understanding of multimodal large language models like PaliGemma2 with the localized feature extraction capabilities of vision-only models like DINOv2. This hybrid design leverages the complementary strengths of both models, enabling robust detection of subtle manipulations while maintaining interpretability. Extensive experiments on diverse datasets demonstrate that TruthLens outperforms state-of-the-art methods in detection accuracy (by 2-14%) and explainability, in both in-domain and cross-data settings, generalizing effectively across traditional and emerging manipulation techniques.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TruthLens, a novel framework for explainable DeepFake detection, addressing both face-manipulated and fully AI-generated content.  TruthLens goes beyond binary classification (real/fake) by providing detailed textual reasoning for its predictions, answering nuanced queries about specific facial features. The architecture combines the global contextual understanding of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) like PaliGemma2 with the localized feature extraction capabilities of vision-only models like DINOv2. This hybrid design is fine-tuned to improve detection accuracy and provide interpretable explanations, demonstrated through experiments on diverse datasets, showing improvements over state-of-the-art methods in both detection accuracy and explainability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its unified approach to DeepFake detection, handling both traditional face manipulations and fully synthetic images *with explanations*. While existing methods often specialize in one category or lack interpretability, TruthLens combines an MLLM and a vision-only model to achieve both accuracy and detailed reasoning. The mixture of feature (MoF) strategy that interleaved and concatenated tokens from both the language and vision models is novel.

*   **Significance:** DeepFake detection is a crucial and growing field. The ability to not only detect but *explain* the detection is highly valuable, especially in scenarios requiring human understanding and trust in AI systems. The approach of fine-tuning a general MLLM with visual features and local textures opens avenues for detecting subtle and unseen manipulation techniques in the wild.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Unified Approach:** Addresses both face-manipulated and fully synthetic content, a gap in existing literature.
    *   **Explainability:** Provides textual explanations for its decisions, enhancing transparency and trust.
    *   **Hybrid Architecture:** Cleverly combines the strengths of MLLMs and vision-only models. The MoF approach has improved accuracy.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** Demonstrates superior performance compared to SOTA methods on diverse datasets.
    *   **Detailed Ablation Studies:** provides insights into the impact of model components (e.g., feature mixing strategies, adapter training).

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependence on Foundation Models:** The performance heavily relies on the capabilities of the underlying PaliGemma2 and DINOv2 models. While this approach enables leveraging existing knowledge, it also inherits any biases or limitations of these models.
    *   **Complexity:** The architecture is complex, involving pretraining and fine-tuning stages. More lightweight solutions may be preferred in resource-constrained environments.
    *   **Evaluation Metric Rigor:** The use of Gemini-1.0 as an LLM-as-a-judge introduces potential biases depending on the prompts used. While it's a valid approach, ensuring the metric's impartiality is crucial.

*   **Potential Impact:**

    *   **Advancing DeepFake Detection:** The proposed framework has the potential to significantly improve the accuracy and reliability of DeepFake detection systems.
    *   **Promoting Trustworthy AI:** By providing explanations for its decisions, TruthLens can help build trust in AI systems and facilitate informed decision-making.
    *   **Inspiring Further Research:**  The work highlights the benefits of combining MLLMs and vision-only models, which can inspire further research in this area.
    *   **Addressing a Critical Societal Problem:**  Addresses the growing threat of malicious and misleading content.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the novelty of its approach, its performance in handling both manipulated and synthetic content, its textual explanations, and strong experimental results, the paper makes a substantial contribution to the field. However, the reliance on powerful foundation models, the metric, and potential complexity temper the assessment slightly.  It's a solid advancement but leaves room for further optimization, simplification, and evaluation metrics.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### UniCoRN: Latent Diffusion-based Unified Controllable Image Restoration Network across Multiple Degradations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15868v1)
- **Authors**: Debabrata Mandal, Soumitri Chattopadhyay, Guansen Tong, Praneeth Chakravarthula
- **Abstract**: Image restoration is essential for enhancing degraded images across computer vision tasks. However, most existing methods address only a single type of degradation (e.g., blur, noise, or haze) at a time, limiting their real-world applicability where multiple degradations often occur simultaneously. In this paper, we propose UniCoRN, a unified image restoration approach capable of handling multiple degradation types simultaneously using a multi-head diffusion model. Specifically, we uncover the potential of low-level visual cues extracted from images in guiding a controllable diffusion model for real-world image restoration and we design a multi-head control network adaptable via a mixture-of-experts strategy. We train our model without any prior assumption of specific degradations, through a smartly designed curriculum learning recipe. Additionally, we also introduce MetaRestore, a metalens imaging benchmark containing images with multiple degradations and artifacts. Extensive evaluations on several challenging datasets, including our benchmark, demonstrate that our method achieves significant performance gains and can robustly restore images with severe degradations. Project page: https://codejaeger.github.io/unicorn-gh
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces UNICORN, a unified and controllable image restoration network based on latent diffusion models. UNICORN aims to address the challenge of restoring images with multiple, unknown degradations simultaneously. The approach utilizes low-level visual cues, extracted from the degraded input, to guide a controllable diffusion model for image restoration. This is achieved via a multi-head control network, adapted using a mixture-of-experts strategy. The model is trained using a curriculum learning approach to progressively handle single and compound degradations. The paper also contributes METARESTORE, a new metalens imaging benchmark dataset containing images with multiple real-world degradations and artifacts. Extensive experiments demonstrate UNICORN's robust performance on existing benchmarks and its ability to restore severely degraded images, especially on the METARESTORE dataset.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its unified approach to multi-degradation image restoration using controllable diffusion models guided by low-level visual cues. While the individual components (diffusion models, control networks, curriculum learning) are not entirely new, their integration into a cohesive framework for handling multiple *unknown* degradations represents a significant advancement. The incorporation of metalens captured real-world dataset is also a notable contribution. Existing methods typically focus on single degradation types or require prior knowledge of the specific degradations present. UNICORN's ability to adapt to multiple degradations without prior knowledge is a key differentiator. However, there is overlap with prior work in universal image restoration. The difference comes from using of low-level features and curriculum learning.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial gap in image restoration research: the ability to handle complex, real-world degradations that often involve multiple factors. The creation of the METARESTORE dataset further enhances the significance of the work, providing a challenging benchmark for future research in this area. The demonstrated performance gains over existing methods, particularly on METARESTORE, suggest that UNICORN has the potential to significantly improve image restoration in practical applications where degradations are unpredictable. This is especially applicable to domains like autonomous vehicles, underwater imaging, and restoration of images captured with low-quality optics. The approach to curriculum learning is significant as it addresses catastrophic forgetting, a common problem when learning multiple tasks.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Unified framework for handling multiple unknown degradations.
    *   Effective use of low-level visual cues for controllable diffusion-based restoration.
    *   Introduction of the METARESTORE dataset, providing a challenging real-world benchmark.
    *   Demonstrated robust performance on diverse datasets, outperforming existing methods.
    * Task-hierarchical Curriculum Learning strategy to prevent catastrophic forgetting.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the paper demonstrates impressive results, the computational cost of diffusion models remains a concern. The inference time is relatively high compared to non-diffusion-based approaches. However, authors show that it is 3-5x faster than other diffusion based models.
    *   The selection of low-level visual cues could be further justified. While the chosen cues appear effective, a more thorough analysis of their individual contributions and potential alternatives would strengthen the paper.
    *   The quantitative results could be improved with more modern and complex baselines.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research in image restoration, particularly in the areas of unified degradation handling, controllable diffusion models, and the use of low-level visual cues. The METARESTORE dataset will serve as a valuable resource for the community, driving further progress in addressing real-world degradation challenges. The work highlights the importance of moving beyond single-degradation assumptions and developing more robust and adaptable restoration techniques.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

UNICORN presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of image restoration. It effectively addresses the limitations of existing methods by providing a unified framework for handling multiple unknown degradations. The approach is well-motivated, technically sound, and supported by comprehensive experimental results. The creation of the METARESTORE dataset further enhances the paper's significance and potential impact. However, the relatively high computational cost and limitations in comparison to more recent baselines prevent it from achieving a higher score. Therefore, the paper deserves a score of 7.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### MASH-VLM: Mitigating Action-Scene Hallucination in Video-LLMs through Disentangled Spatial-Temporal Representations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15871v1)
- **Authors**: Kyungho Bae, Jinhyung Kim, Sihaeng Lee, Soonyoung Lee, Gunhee Lee, Jinwoo Choi
- **Abstract**: In this work, we tackle action-scene hallucination in Video Large Language Models (Video-LLMs), where models incorrectly predict actions based on the scene context or scenes based on observed actions. We observe that existing Video-LLMs often suffer from action-scene hallucination due to two main factors. First, existing Video-LLMs intermingle spatial and temporal features by applying an attention operation across all tokens. Second, they use the standard Rotary Position Embedding (RoPE), which causes the text tokens to overemphasize certain types of tokens depending on their sequential orders. To address these issues, we introduce MASH-VLM, Mitigating Action-Scene Hallucination in Video-LLMs through disentangled spatial-temporal representations. Our approach includes two key innovations: (1) DST-attention, a novel attention mechanism that disentangles the spatial and temporal tokens within the LLM by using masked attention to restrict direct interactions between the spatial and temporal tokens; (2) Harmonic-RoPE, which extends the dimensionality of the positional IDs, allowing the spatial and temporal tokens to maintain balanced positions relative to the text tokens. To evaluate the action-scene hallucination in Video-LLMs, we introduce the UNSCENE benchmark with 1,320 videos and 4,078 QA pairs. Extensive experiments demonstrate that MASH-VLM achieves state-of-the-art results on the UNSCENE benchmark, as well as on existing video understanding benchmarks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MASH-VLM, a novel approach to mitigate action-scene hallucination in Video Large Language Models (Video-LLMs). The core idea is to disentangle spatial and temporal representations within the LLM. This is achieved through two main components: DST-attention, which uses masked attention to prevent direct interactions between spatial and temporal tokens, and Harmonic-RoPE, which assigns balanced positional IDs to spatial and temporal tokens. The authors also introduce a new benchmark, UNSCENE, specifically designed to evaluate action-scene hallucination. Experimental results demonstrate that MASH-VLM achieves state-of-the-art performance on the UNSCENE benchmark and other video understanding benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonably novel approach to a significant problem in Video-LLMs: action-scene hallucination. The combination of DST-attention and Harmonic-RoPE is a unique way of disentangling spatial and temporal information *within* the LLM.  Prior works often focused on disentangling inputs *before* feeding them into the model, making this a distinct contribution. The UNSCENE benchmark is also a valuable contribution, as it directly addresses the specific issue of action-scene hallucination, which was previously lacking in quantitative benchmarks. However, the specific techniques (masked attention, modified RoPE) are inspired by prior work on disentanglement and positional embeddings, limiting the fundamental novelty.

*   **Significance:** Action-scene hallucination is a critical issue that undermines the reliability of Video-LLMs. MASH-VLM shows a substantial improvement in mitigating this issue, especially on the UNSCENE benchmark. This makes the work significant, as it moves the field closer to building more trustworthy and reliable Video-LLMs. The comprehensive experiments on various benchmarks further strengthen the claims. The thorough ablation studies provide insights into the effectiveness of each component (DST-attention, Harmonic-RoPE), further increasing the significance. However, the qualitative results, while supportive, could benefit from more varied examples.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Novel architecture that disentangles spatial and temporal features *within* the LLM.
    *   Introduction of a targeted benchmark (UNSCENE).
    *   Strong empirical results on various benchmarks, demonstrating SOTA performance.
    *   Comprehensive ablation studies that provide insights into each component.
    *   Well-written and easy to follow.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The individual components (masked attention, modified RoPE) are inspired by existing techniques, limiting fundamental novelty.
    *   Qualitative analysis could be improved with more examples illustrating different types of failure modes.
    *   The reliance on GPT-3.5 for open-ended question evaluation, though standard, introduces a potential bias.
    *   The computational cost of the method compared to baseline should be elaborated

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence future research on Video-LLMs by highlighting the importance of disentangling spatial and temporal representations. The UNSCENE benchmark could become a standard evaluation tool for assessing action-scene hallucination. The MASH-VLM architecture could serve as a template for future models aiming to improve the reliability of video understanding.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of Video-LLMs by addressing the critical issue of action-scene hallucination. The combination of DST-attention and Harmonic-RoPE provides a unique way of disentangling spatial and temporal information, and the introduction of the UNSCENE benchmark allows for targeted evaluation. The experimental results and ablation studies are comprehensive and support the claims. However, the incremental nature of some of the individual components, the limitations of the qualitative analysis, and absence of computing cost analysis prevent it from receiving a higher score. While the techniques used are not revolutionary on their own, their combined application and the creation of a new dataset to resolve this specific problem make this paper a solid contribution to the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### DeepPsy-Agent: A Stage-Aware and Deep-Thinking Emotional Support Agent System
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15876v1)
- **Authors**: Kai Chen, Zebing Sun
- **Abstract**: This paper introduces DeepPsy-Agent, an innovative psychological support system that combines the three-stage helping theory in psychology with deep learning techniques. The system consists of two core components: (1) a multi-stage response-capable dialogue model (\textit{deeppsy-chat}), which enhances reasoning capabilities through stage-awareness and deep-thinking analysis to generate high-quality responses; and (2) a real-time stage transition detection model that identifies contextual shifts to guide the dialogue towards more effective intervention stages. Based on 30,000 real psychological hotline conversations, we employ AI-simulated dialogues and expert re-annotation strategies to construct a high-quality multi-turn dialogue dataset. Experimental results demonstrate that DeepPsy-Agent outperforms general-purpose large language models (LLMs) in key metrics such as problem exposure completeness, cognitive restructuring success rate, and action adoption rate. Ablation studies further validate the effectiveness of stage-awareness and deep-thinking modules, showing that stage information contributes 42.3\% to performance, while the deep-thinking module increases root-cause identification by 58.3\% and reduces ineffective suggestions by 72.1\%. This system addresses critical challenges in AI-based psychological support through dynamic dialogue management and deep reasoning, advancing intelligent mental health services.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DeepPsy-Agent, an AI-based emotional support system designed to enhance mental health services. It integrates Professor Clara Hill's three-stage Exploration-Insight-Action model from psychology with deep learning techniques.  The system features a multi-stage dialogue model (deeppsy-chat) and a real-time stage transition detection model. DeepPsy-Chat is trained on a dataset of 30,000 real psychological hotline conversations, augmented by AI-simulated dialogues and expert re-annotations.  The system dynamically adjusts its responses based on dialogue context and reasoning capabilities. The experiments show DeepPsy-Agent outperforms general-purpose LLMs in problem exposure, cognitive restructuring, and action adoption. Ablation studies confirmed the importance of both stage-awareness and deep-thinking modules.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper's novelty lies primarily in its specific *integration* of a well-established psychological theory (Hill's Exploration-Insight-Action model) with modern deep learning techniques, especially in a dialogue system for emotional support.  While using psychological principles in AI is not entirely new, and LLMs have been applied to mental health tasks before, the explicit design based on Hill's model and the real-time stage transition detection component are fairly novel. The specific approach of generating synthetic dialogues and re-annotating them by experts to achieve a better training dataset is a contribution too.

**Significance:**

The significance stems from the potential to create more effective and personalized emotional support systems.  Current LLM-based solutions often lack the nuance and theoretical grounding to provide truly helpful guidance. By explicitly modeling the different stages of emotional support, the system aims to overcome the limitations of generic LLMs. The improvements in key metrics like problem exposure and cognitive restructuring suggest a tangible benefit compared to baseline models. If effectively deployed, such a system could increase accessibility and improve the quality of mental health support.

**Strengths:**

*   **Sound Theoretical Foundation:** The system is explicitly based on a respected psychological theory.
*   **Comprehensive Experimental Evaluation:** The paper provides a thorough evaluation with multiple baselines, ablation studies, and case studies.
*   **Explicit Stage Modeling:** The inclusion of stage transition detection and stage-aware dialogue generation seems crucial for providing contextually relevant support.
*   **Data Augmentation Strategy:** The combination of real-world data, AI-simulated conversations and expert re-annotation is a valuable data creation strategy.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Generalizability:**  The system is trained on a specific dataset of hotline conversations. The effectiveness in other contexts (e.g., different age groups, cultures, or severity of mental health issues) needs to be evaluated.
*   **Ethical Considerations:** The paper acknowledges AI support but doesn't explicitly discuss safety mechanism/red-teaming, privacy, and bias issues thoroughly. It is vital to prevent AI from causing further harm or replacing human experts.
*   **Lack of User Studies:** While quantitative metrics show improvement, the subjective experience of users interacting with the system isn't explored. User feedback is vital to assess if the AI truly "understands" them.
*   **Specific LLM Dependence:** The reliance on particular LLMs (qwq and qwen) means the system's performance is tied to these models. What about others? This makes it less reusable than if the LLM choice were a simple hyperparameter.
* **Overstated Novelty:** Integration of well-defined psychological frameworks isn't exceptionally novel in itself as several other works have attempted the same.

**Justification of Score:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses, the paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of AI-based emotional support. The clear link to established psychological theory and empirical validation justify a positive assessment. However, several limitations regarding generalizability, ethical concerns, and subjective user experience prevent it from receiving a higher score. The significance is real, but the novelty is a bit over-stated. While the specific integration is innovative, similar ideas have been explored previously.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Repurposing 2D Diffusion Models with Gaussian Atlas for 3D Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15877v1)
- **Authors**: Tiange Xiang, Kai Li, Chengjiang Long, Christian Häne, Peihong Guo, Scott Delp, Ehsan Adeli, Li Fei-Fei
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in text-to-image diffusion models have been driven by the increasing availability of paired 2D data. However, the development of 3D diffusion models has been hindered by the scarcity of high-quality 3D data, resulting in less competitive performance compared to their 2D counterparts. To address this challenge, we propose repurposing pre-trained 2D diffusion models for 3D object generation. We introduce Gaussian Atlas, a novel representation that utilizes dense 2D grids, enabling the fine-tuning of 2D diffusion models to generate 3D Gaussians. Our approach demonstrates successful transfer learning from a pre-trained 2D diffusion model to a 2D manifold flattened from 3D structures. To support model training, we compile GaussianVerse, a large-scale dataset comprising 205K high-quality 3D Gaussian fittings of various 3D objects. Our experimental results show that text-to-image diffusion models can be effectively adapted for 3D content generation, bridging the gap between 2D and 3D modeling.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Repurposing 2D Diffusion Models with Gaussian Atlas for 3D Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of limited high-quality 3D data hindering the performance of 3D diffusion models compared to their 2D counterparts.  The authors propose a method to repurpose pre-trained 2D diffusion models for 3D object generation. They introduce "Gaussian Atlas," a novel 2D representation of 3D Gaussians that facilitates fine-tuning 2D diffusion models to generate 3D structures.  To support training, they create a large-scale dataset called "GaussianVerse" containing high-quality 3D Gaussian fittings.  Experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of adapting text-to-image diffusion models for 3D content generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The core idea of converting 3D Gaussians to a 2D representation (Gaussian Atlas) to leverage pre-trained 2D diffusion models is reasonably novel.  While prior works explored using 2D priors for 3D generation (e.g., Triplanes), the Gaussian Atlas approach offers a different structuralization that attempts to maintain some 3D geometric information in the 2D mapping. The method provides a simple and deterministic function compared with training neural networks.  The creation of a large-scale dataset of 3D Gaussian fittings (GaussianVerse) is also a useful contribution.

**Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its potential to bridge the gap between the mature field of 2D generative models and the developing field of 3D generative models. The efficient and effective 3D content creation unlocked by leveraging learned 2D priors opens new possibilities. Overcoming data scarcity in 3D generative modeling is crucial for numerous real-world applications. The paper demonstrates an improved generation prompt alignment, which is important. The user studies also back up the improvements of their methods.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly identifies the limitations in 3D generative modeling and proposes a targeted solution.
*   **Novel Approach:** The Gaussian Atlas representation is a clever way to structure 3D data to be compatible with 2D models.
*   **Large-Scale Dataset:** The creation of GaussianVerse is a significant undertaking and provides a valuable resource for the community.
*   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experiments demonstrate improved performance compared to existing 3D generation methods, as validated through qualitative and quantitative metrics and user studies.
*   **Well-written and Well-organized:** The paper is generally well-structured and easy to follow.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited comparison with SOTA:** Direct comparisons with other related works that also adopt 2D representations of 3D content (PI3D, HexaGen3D) are missing due to lack of publicly available implementations.
*   **Dependence on a specific LD model:** The paper is tied to a specific LD model and its limitations are inherited.
*   **Limited novelty on post-processes:** There is not any details on whether a new algorithm is used to refine generated models.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper's approach could influence future research in 3D generative modeling by demonstrating the benefits of leveraging 2D priors. The Gaussian Atlas representation could inspire other methods for encoding 3D information in a 2D-compatible format. The GaussianVerse dataset is likely to be used by other researchers in the field.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper makes a reasonably novel and significant contribution to the field of 3D generative modeling. It presents a technically sound approach, backed by a large-scale dataset and compelling experimental results. While there are some limitations, such as dependance on specific LD models and a few missing comparisons with SOTA, the paper's overall impact on the field is positive. The proposed Gaussian Atlas bridges the gap between mature 2D generation and relatively novel 3D generation.
